user
You are an adversarial code reviewer. Review the CURRENT working tree of this repo (branch fix/473-reject-auth-error-bodies) focused on src/services/download-cache.ts and its tests src/__tests__/services/download-cache.test.ts and src/__tests__/services/storage.test.ts.

Context: this branch hardens issue #473 — a remote CivitAI / any download that saves an auth/error HTML/JSON body as a .safetensors and reports success. A prior adversarial review found 3 P0s + 1 P1 that this revision claims to fix:

P0-1: valid HTML with a form-feed (\f) control byte still finalized as a model, and Content-Type was ignored once the head was non-empty. Fix must (a) treat TAB/LF/VT/FF/CR as text-compatible whitespace so a \f-HTML page classifies as HTML, and (b) make Content-Type text/html or application/json AUTHORITATIVE for a binary-model destination (reject regardless of a stray control byte).

P0-2: a legitimate raw binary starting with '<' + a printable 64-byte prefix was wrongly REJECTED (false-negative). Fix must require the WHOLE sniffed window to be valid text (or a real structural signature) before classifying html/json, so a raw .bin whose head merely starts with '<' is ACCEPTED.

P0-3: cloud/direct finalize failed OPEN when the payload couldn't be sniffed. Fix must fail CLOSED (cannot verify => do not finalize), same as the HTTP path.

P1: rejection cleanup swallowed all rm errors, leaving a poisoned partial/.etag/cache entry that a retry could resume/re-serve. Fix must surface/log cleanup failure AND ensure a left-behind poisoned partial can't be treated as resumable on retry.

Verify each P0/P1 is genuinely fixed with no regression to the #467 hardening (resume/If-Range/redirect/size gates) or the working parts (ordinary <html>/JSON rejection, UTF-8/UTF-16 detection, GGUF/safetensors-0x3C-header acceptance). Look hard for NEW correctness bugs, false-positives that would reject legitimate downloads, and false-negatives that would still let an auth/error body finalize.

Output your verdict. The VERY FIRST LINE of your response MUST be exactly "VERDICT: PASS" or "VERDICT: FAIL" (no other text on that line). Then explain, listing any concrete P0/P1 issues with file:line.

+
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+// #473: an auth/error response BODY (HTML login page / JSON error) must NEVER be
+// finalized as a `.safetensors` (or any binary-model) file under a false success.
+// Before this fix a 200-with-HTML/JSON body of plausible size passed the #467
+// size gate and landed as a corrupt model. These drive the content-type/magic-byte
+// validation: the offending download FAILS with an actionable error and NO file is
+// finalized, while a legitimate binary body still succeeds.
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+describe("downloadModel model-payload validation (#473)", () => {
+  const HTML_AUTH_PAGE =
+    "<!DOCTYPE html>\n<html><head><title>Sign in</title></head>" +
+    "<body><form>Please log in to CivitAI to download this model.</form></body></html>";
+  const JSON_ERROR = '{"error":"Unauthorized","message":"An API key is required to download this asset."}';
+
+  async function expectRejectedAndNoFile(
+    url: string,
+    subfolder: string,
+    filename: string,
+    response: Response,
+  ) {
+    fetchMock.mockResolvedValueOnce(response);
+    await expect(downloadModel(url, subfolder, filename)).rejects.toThrow(/not a model file|model file/i);
+    // Nothing finalized in the models dir…
+    const modelsDir = join(comfyDir, "models", subfolder);
+    const landed = await readdir(modelsDir).catch(() => [] as string[]);
+    expect(landed).toHaveLength(0);
+    // …and nothing re-servable left in the cache. NOTE: a CivitAI API download URL
+    // (…/api/download/models/<id>) is EXTENSIONLESS, so its cache file has no
+    // `.safetensors` suffix — an `endsWith(".safetensors")` check would miss a
+    // poisoned entry entirely. Assert instead that NO finalized cache entry remains
+    // (any non-dot file) and that any leftover artifact is a neutralized 0-byte file.
+    const cached = await readdir(cacheDir).catch(() => [] as string[]);
+    const finalized = cached.filter((f) => !f.startsWith("."));
+    expect(finalized).toHaveLength(0);
+    for (const f of cached) {
+      expect((await stat(join(cacheDir, f))).size).toBe(0);
+    }
+  }
+
+  it("rejects an HTML auth-challenge body saved as .safetensors (text/html)", async () => {
+    await expectRejectedAndNoFile(
+      "https://civitai.com/api/download/models/3174878",
+      "loras",
+      "gated.safetensors",
+      new Response(HTML_AUTH_PAGE, {
+        status: 200,
+        statusText: "OK",
+        headers: { "content-type": "text/html; charset=utf-8" },
+      }),
+    );
+  });
+
+  it("rejects an HTML auth-challenge body even when mislabeled as octet-stream (magic bytes win)", async () => {
+    await expectRejectedAndNoFile(
+      "https://example.com/models/mislabeled.safetensors",
+      "checkpoints",
+      "mislabeled.safetensors",
+      new Response(HTML_AUTH_PAGE, {
+        status: 200,
+        statusText: "OK",
+        headers: { "content-type": "application/octet-stream" },
+      }),
+    );
+  });
+
+  it("rejects a UTF-16LE HTML auth page saved as .safetensors", async () => {
+    // A real auth page can be served UTF-16 (bytes FF FE 3C 00 …). Body magic must
+    // decode the BOM and still detect the HTML (codex finding).
+    const utf16 = Buffer.concat([
+      Buffer.from([0xff, 0xfe]), // UTF-16LE BOM
+      Buffer.from(HTML_AUTH_PAGE, "utf16le"),
+    ]);
+    await expectRejectedAndNoFile(
+      "https://example.com/models/u16.safetensors",
+      "checkpoints",
+      "u16.safetensors",
+      new Response(utf16, {
+        status: 200,
+        statusText: "OK",
+        headers: { "content-type": "text/html; charset=utf-16le" },
+      }),
+    );
+  });
+
+  it("rejects a JSON error body saved as .safetensors", async () => {
+    await expectRejectedAndNoFile(
+      "https://civitai.com/api/download/models/999",
+      "loras",
+      "err.safetensors",
+      new Response(JSON_ERROR, {
+        status: 200,
+        statusText: "OK",
+        headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
+      }),
+    );
+  });
+
+  it("rejects an HTML body saved as .pt2 (a non-safetensors binary model format)", async () => {
+    await expectRejectedAndNoFile(
+      "https://example.com/models/x.pt2",
+      "checkpoints",

+      new Response(HTML_AUTH_PAGE, {

+/** Remove `path`, returning true iff it is confirmed gone. Unlike `safeRm` this
+ *  LOGS a removal failure (EPERM/EACCES/…) instead of swallowing it silently — a
+ *  left-behind rejected artifact (a poisoned partial / cache entry) must at least be
+ *  visible, since a later cache-hit or resume could otherwise re-hit it (#473 P1). */
+async function rmOrLog(path: string, logUrl: string): Promise<boolean> {
+  try {
+    await rm(path, { force: true });
+    return true;
+  } catch (err) {
+    logger.warn(
+      `Failed to remove a rejected download artifact "${path}" — a retry may re-hit it; ` +
+        `delete it manually if the same rejection repeats.`,
+      { url: logUrl, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) },
+    );
+    return false;
+  }
+}
+
+/** Discard a payload that was REJECTED as a non-model (HTML/JSON auth/error) body.
+ *  Removes the file and its resume sidecar and, CRUCIALLY, if the file itself can't
+ *  be removed, NEUTRALIZES it by truncating to 0 bytes so a retry can neither resume
+ *  onto the poisoned prefix (a 0-byte partial is treated as fresh) nor serve it as a
+ *  cache hit (a 0-byte cache entry is treated as a miss). Surfaces a hard failure via
+ *  the log when it can neither remove nor truncate (#473 P1). */
+async function discardRejectedPayload(
+  path: string,
+  sidecarPath: string | undefined,
+  logUrl: string,
+): Promise<void> {
+  const removed = await rmOrLog(path, logUrl);
+  if (sidecarPath) await rmOrLog(sidecarPath, logUrl);
+  if (removed) return;
+  // Removal failed — neutralize the still-present poisoned bytes so a retry can't
+  // resume onto them or re-serve them as a cache hit.
+  try {
+    await writeFile(path, "");
+  } catch (err) {
+    logger.error(
+      `Could not remove OR truncate a rejected non-model payload "${path}"; a retry may re-serve ` +
+        `or resume it. Delete this file manually.`,
+      { url: logUrl, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) },
+    );
+  }
+}
+
 /** On-disk size, or undefined when it can't be determined (fs layer stubbed in
  *  tests, or a stat hiccup) — callers must not block a download over a missing
  *  number, only over a number that proves corruption. */
@@ -55,6 +101,313 @@ async function fileSizeOrUndefined(path: string): Promise<number | undefined> {
   }
 }
 
+/** Destination extensions whose bytes MUST be a binary model payload — never an
+ *  HTML page or a JSON document. A download that lands under one of these but whose
+ *  body is actually an auth-challenge / error page is the #473 false-success class:
+ *  a login HTML or `{"error":...}` JSON saved as `.safetensors`, reported as a green
+ *  success, then failing at load time ("header too large"). Extensions NOT in this
+ *  set (e.g. `.json`, `.yaml`, `.txt`, config sidecars) are left unvalidated so a
+ *  legitimate text/JSON download is never rejected. */
+// Superset of every BINARY model format the repo recognizes as a downloadable
+// weight file — kept in sync with the recognizers in missing-models.ts (MODEL_EXTS)
+// and workflow-converter.ts (the model-file regex). Intentionally EXCLUDES the
+// textual formats those recognizers also allow (`.yaml`, `.json`): those legitimately
+// contain text, so validating them as binary would reject a valid download.
+const MODEL_BINARY_EXTS = new Set([
+  ".safetensors",
+  ".safetensor",
+  ".sft",
+  ".ckpt",
+  ".pt",
+  ".pt2",
+  ".pth",
+  ".bin",
+  ".gguf",
+  ".onnx",
+  ".vae",
+  ".pkl",
+  ".npz",
+  ".msgpack",
+]);
+
+/** How many leading bytes to sniff for a payload's shape. Enough to see an HTML
+ *  doctype/tag or a JSON error envelope's opening structure without reading a
+ *  multi-GB model in full. */
+const PAYLOAD_SNIFF_BYTES = 512;
+
+/** Read the first `n` bytes of a file WITHOUT loading the whole thing (models are
+ *  multi-GB) — used to sniff a completed download's shape. Best-effort: returns
+ *  undefined if the file can't be opened/read (never blocks a download over a read
+ *  hiccup — the size gate has already run). */
+async function readHead(path: string, n = PAYLOAD_SNIFF_BYTES): Promise<Buffer | undefined> {
+  let fh: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof open>> | undefined;
+  try {
+    fh = await open(path, "r");
+    const buf = Buffer.alloc(n);
+    // Loop: a single read() can return fewer bytes than requested even when more
+    // are available; fill up to n (or EOF) so a short first read can't truncate the
+    // sniff window and hide an HTML/JSON body after byte ~1.
+    let off = 0;
+    while (off < n) {
+      const { bytesRead } = await fh.read(buf, off, n - off, off);
+      if (bytesRead === 0) break; // EOF
+      off += bytesRead;
+    }
+    return buf.subarray(0, off);
+  } catch {
+    return undefined;
+  } finally {
+    await fh?.close().catch(() => undefined);
+  }
+}
+
+/** True when a run of bytes reads as a valid UTF-8 TEXT document (what an HTML page
+ *  or JSON error IS) rather than binary model bytes. This is a real UTF-8 validation,
+ *  not "any high byte counts as text": each non-ASCII byte must be a well-formed
+ *  UTF-8 lead followed by valid continuation bytes, and NUL / C0 control bytes
+ *  (except the text whitespace controls TAB/LF/VT/FF/CR) hard-fail. Random binary that happens to start with `<`/`{`/`[`
+ *  almost immediately hits an invalid UTF-8 lead byte (0x80–0xC1 and 0xF5–0xFF — ~26%
+ *  of all byte values) or a control byte, so it is correctly kept binary; a safetensors
+ *  whose LE header length is 60 (0x3C='<') is followed by NUL padding and fails at once.
+ *  A multibyte sequence truncated by the end of the sniff window is accepted (we only
+ *  sampled a prefix), never treated as invalid. */
+function looksLikeText(slice: Buffer): boolean {
+  const n = slice.length;
+  if (n === 0) return false;
+  let i = 0;
+  while (i < n) {
+    const b = slice[i];
+    if (b < 0x80) {
+      // ASCII: printable + the standard text whitespace controls TAB(0x09) LF(0x0a)
+      // VT(0x0b) FF(0x0c) CR(0x0d). A form-feed / vertical-tab is valid HTML/XML
+      // whitespace, so a real login page like `<html>\f<body>…` MUST still read as
+      // text (else it slips through as "binary" and finalizes as a model — #473
+      // P0-1). Any OTHER C0 control (NUL etc.) ⇒ not text.
+      if ((b >= 0x09 && b <= 0x0d) || (b >= 0x20 && b <= 0x7e)) {
+        i += 1;
+        continue;
+      }
+      return false;
+    }
+    // Multibyte lead byte → determine the sequence length; invalid leads reject.
+    let len: number;
+    if (b >= 0xc2 && b <= 0xdf) len = 2;
+    else if (b >= 0xe0 && b <= 0xef) len = 3;
+    else if (b >= 0xf0 && b <= 0xf4) len = 4;
+    else return false; // 0x80–0xC1 (bare continuation / overlong) or 0xF5–0xFF
+    if (i + len > n) return true; // sequence truncated by the sniff window — accept
+    // Strict continuation ranges reject overlong / surrogate / out-of-range forms:
+    //   E0 → A0..BF (else overlong), ED → 80..9F (else surrogate),
+    //   F0 → 90..BF (else overlong), F4 → 80..8F (else > U+10FFFF).
+    let lo = 0x80;
+    let hi = 0xbf;
+    if (b === 0xe0) lo = 0xa0;
+    else if (b === 0xed) hi = 0x9f;
+    else if (b === 0xf0) lo = 0x90;
+    else if (b === 0xf4) hi = 0x8f;
+    if (slice[i + 1] < lo || slice[i + 1] > hi) return false;
+    for (let k = 2; k < len; k += 1) {
+      const cont = slice[i + k];
+      if (cont < 0x80 || cont > 0xbf) return false; // not a valid continuation byte
+    }
+    i += len;
+  }
+  return true;
+}
+
+/** Classify a sniffed payload head as an HTML page, a JSON document, or opaque
+ *  binary. Skips a UTF-8 BOM and leading ASCII whitespace, then requires the run
+ *  that follows to read as TEXT before declaring html/json — so a binary model that
+ *  merely starts with byte `<`/`{`/`[` (e.g. a safetensors whose LE header length is
+ *  60=0x3C or 91=0x5B, followed by NUL padding) is correctly kept as "binary":
+ *   - leading `<` + text ⇒ "html"
+ *   - leading `{`/`[` + text ⇒ "json"
+ *   - anything else ⇒ "binary" (a real model payload). */
+/** Classify an already-DECODED text string (used for UTF-16 bodies): skips leading
+ *  whitespace, then a `<` ⇒ html / `{`|`[` ⇒ json when the run that follows is clean
+ *  text (no NUL / C0 control). A binary payload that merely decoded to a leading
+ *  `<`/`{` (e.g. a safetensors whose UTF-16 decode yields `<` then U+0000) fails the
+ *  text check and stays binary. */
+function classifyDecodedText(s: string): "html" | "json" | "binary" {
+  let j = 0;
+  while (j < s.length && (s[j] === " " || s[j] === "\t" || s[j] === "\n" || s[j] === "\r")) j += 1;
+  if (j >= s.length) return "binary";
+  const ch = s[j];
+  // Require the WHOLE decoded run (not just the first 64 chars) to be clean text
+  // before declaring html/json: a binary payload that merely decoded to a leading
+  // `<`/`{` but then hits control/replacement chars must stay binary (#473 P0-2).
+  const rest = s.slice(j);
+  const isText = (() => {
+    for (const c of rest) {
+      const cp = c.codePointAt(0)!;
+      if (cp >= 0x09 && cp <= 0x0d) continue; // TAB/LF/VT/FF/CR — text whitespace
+      if (cp < 0x20 || cp === 0xfffd) return false; // NUL/C0 control or replacement char
+    }
+    return true;
+  })();
+  if (ch === "<") return isText ? "html" : "binary";
+  if (ch === "{" || ch === "[") return isText ? "json" : "binary";
+  return "binary";
+}
+
+function classifyPayload(buf: Buffer): "html" | "json" | "binary" {
+  // A UTF-16 BOM (FF FE = LE, FE FF = BE) marks a text document — an HTML/JSON auth
+  // page can be served UTF-16 (e.g. `text/html; charset=utf-16`, bytes FF FE 3C 00).
+  // Decode and classify as text so it isn't mistaken for binary. A binary model that
+  // coincidentally starts with a BOM byte pair decodes to control chars → stays binary.
+  if (
+    buf.length >= 2 &&
+    ((buf[0] === 0xff && buf[1] === 0xfe) || (buf[0] === 0xfe && buf[1] === 0xff))
+  ) {
+    try {
+      const label = buf[0] === 0xff ? "utf-16le" : "utf-16be";
+      return classifyDecodedText(new TextDecoder(label).decode(buf));
+    } catch {
+      return "binary";
+    }
+  }
+  let i = 0;
+  if (buf.length >= 3 && buf[0] === 0xef && buf[1] === 0xbb && buf[2] === 0xbf) i = 3; // UTF-8 BOM
+  while (
+    i < buf.length &&
+    (buf[i] === 0x20 || buf[i] === 0x09 || buf[i] === 0x0a || buf[i] === 0x0d)
+  ) {
+    i += 1;
+  }
+  if (i >= buf.length) return "binary";
+  const c = buf[i];
+  // Require the WHOLE sniffed run — the entire remaining head, not just the first 64
+  // bytes — to read as valid text before declaring html/json. A raw binary whose head
+  // merely STARTS with `<`/`{`/`[` but turns to non-text bytes further in (e.g. a .bin
+  // that begins `<AAA…` then has NUL/high bytes) must be ACCEPTED as a model, not
+  // rejected on a one-byte lead over a tiny window (#473 P0-2). A genuine HTML/JSON
+  // auth page is text throughout the sniff window, so it still classifies correctly.
+  const rest = buf.subarray(i);
+  if (c === 0x3c) return looksLikeText(rest) ? "html" : "binary"; // '<'
+  if (c === 0x7b || c === 0x5b) return looksLikeText(rest) ? "json" : "binary"; // '{' or '['
+  return "binary";
+}
+
+/** Given a completed download's sniffed head + Content-Type, decide whether the
+ *  bytes are an auth/error page masquerading as a model file (#473). Returns the
+ *  offending kind, or null when the payload is an acceptable model binary (or the
+ *  destination isn't a model-binary extension, so this validation doesn't apply).
+ *
+ *  Two independent signals, EITHER of which rejects:
+ *   1. Body magic — an HTML page's leading `<` (over a whole-window text run) and a
+ *      JSON error's leading `{`/`[` are present regardless of how the server labels
+ *      the response, so body magic catches an auth/error page even when mislabeled
+ *      `application/octet-stream`. (fetch() transparently decompresses gzip/br, so a
+ *      compressed error page still sniffs as its decoded HTML/JSON here.)
+ *   2. Content-Type — a `text/html`/`application/xhtml` or `application/json` label
+ *      on a binary-model destination is itself proof of an auth/error document, so it
+ *      rejects EVEN when the body failed to sniff as text (a stray control byte, an
+ *      exotic charset, or a truncating proxy — #473 P0-1). Real model hosts never
+ *      label a weight file text/html or application/json, so this can't reject a
+ *      legitimate binary (whose Content-Type is octet-stream or similar). */
+function detectNonModelPayload(
+  head: Buffer | undefined,
+  contentType: string,
+  modelExt: string,
+): "html" | "json" | null {
+  if (!modelExt || !MODEL_BINARY_EXTS.has(modelExt.toLowerCase())) return null;
+  const kind = head ? classifyPayload(head) : "binary";
+  if (kind === "html") return "html";
+  if (kind === "json") return "json";
+  // Content-Type is AUTHORITATIVE for the two textual auth/error shapes. A server
+  // that LABELS a response `text/html`/`application/xhtml` or `application/json` for
+  // a binary-model destination is serving a login/auth or API-error document, never
+  // a weight file — so reject it EVEN when the body itself failed to sniff as text
+  // (a stray control byte, an exotic charset, or a truncating proxy could otherwise
+  // let a genuine HTML/JSON page slip through as "binary" — #473 P0-1). Real model
+  // hosts serve weights as application/octet-stream (or similar), never as text/html
+  // or application/json, so this can't reject a legitimate binary. (The body-magic
+  // checks above already caught the common octet-stream-mislabeled auth page.)
+  if (/text\/html|application\/xhtml/i.test(contentType)) return "html";
+  if (/(^|[/+])json\b/i.test(contentType)) return "json";
+  return null;
+}
+
+/** Actionable error for a rejected auth/error payload (#473). Names the concrete
+ *  failure (an HTML page / JSON error saved as a model) and, for CivitAI, the exact
+ *  fix (set/refresh the API token) — because a 200-with-auth-challenge is precisely
+ *  what CivitAI returns for a missing/invalid key or a gated model, and Manager /
+ *  a bare fetch would otherwise save that page under the `.safetensors` name. */
+function nonModelPayloadError(
+  kind: "html" | "json",
+  url: string,
+  logUrl: string,
+): ModelError {
+  let host = "";
+  try {
+    host = new URL(url).hostname;
+  } catch {
+    /* logUrl is used for reporting; host is only for the CivitAI hint */
+  }
+  const isCivitai = /(^|\.)civitai\.com$/i.test(host);
+  const what =
+    kind === "html"
+      ? "an HTML page (an authentication/login or error page)"
+      : "a JSON document (an API error or auth challenge)";
+  const civitaiHint = isCivitai
+    ? " CivitAI returns an auth challenge like this when no valid token is sent — set or refresh " +
+      "CIVITAI_API_TOKEN (panel Settings › “Set CivitAI token…”, or the env var; create one at " +
+      "civitai.com/user/account) and retry. Note: on a REMOTE ComfyUI target the token is NOT " +
+      "forwarded to ComfyUI-Manager, so a locally-set token does not fix a remote Manager download."
+    : "";
+  return new ModelError(
+    `Download rejected: the server returned ${what}, not a model file, but the destination is a ` +
+      `binary model file. Refusing to save it as a model (it would land as a corrupt file under a ` +
+      `false success and fail at load time, e.g. "header too large").${civitaiHint}`,
+    { url: logUrl },
+  );
+}
+
+/** Validate that a COMPLETED file at `targetPath` is a real model payload — not an
+ *  HTML/JSON auth/error page — when the destination is a binary-model extension
+ *  (#473). FAILS CLOSED: an offending payload OR an unreadable-for-sniffing file (we
+ *  can't confirm it) throws, after `onReject` cleans up, so a corrupt file is never
+ *  finalized under a success. A no-op for non-model destinations. `contentType` is a
+ *  secondary signal (empty on cache-hit/cloud paths, where only the bytes are known). */
+async function assertModelPayloadOrThrow(opts: {
+  targetPath: string;
+  modelExt: string;
+  contentType: string;
+  url: string;
+  logUrl: string;
+  onReject?: () => Promise<void>;
+  /** Defaults to TRUE for every finalize path (HTTP, cache-hit, cloud S3/Azure): an
+   *  unreadable/empty sniff head leaves the payload UNVERIFIED and is REJECTED —
+   *  cannot verify ⇒ do not finalize (#473 P0-3). (A genuinely 0-byte download is
+   *  already rejected upstream by assertComplete / the cache-hit 0-byte guard, so
+   *  fail-closed here only trips on a nonzero file we couldn't read.) The flag is kept
+   *  as a seam for callers/tests that need best-effort behavior, but no production
+   *  path passes false. */
+  failClosed?: boolean;
+}): Promise<void> {
+  const { targetPath, modelExt, contentType, url, logUrl, failClosed = true } = opts;
+  if (!modelExt || !MODEL_BINARY_EXTS.has(modelExt.toLowerCase())) return;
+  const head = await readHead(targetPath);
+  // An unreadable OR unexpectedly-empty head (a non-empty file that read 0 bytes
+  // under an fs race) leaves the payload UNVERIFIED. On the fail-closed paths refuse
+  // to finalize it rather than trust it (#473/#467); on the best-effort cloud path,
+  // skip. (A genuinely 0-byte download is already rejected upstream by assertComplete
+  // / the cache-hit 0-byte guard.)
+  if (head === undefined || head.length === 0) {
+    if (!failClosed) return;
+    await opts.onReject?.();
+    throw new ModelError(
+      `Download could not be verified: the completed file could not be read to confirm it is a ` +
+        `model payload (and not an HTML/JSON auth/error page). Not finalizing it as a model; retry.`,
+      { url: logUrl },
+    );
+  }
+  const kind = detectNonModelPayload(head, contentType, modelExt);
+  if (!kind) return;
+  await opts.onReject?.();
+  throw nonModelPayloadError(kind, url, logUrl);
+}
+

78:  if (removed) return;
79:  // Removal failed — neutralize the still-present poisoned bytes so a retry can't
80:  // resume onto them or re-serve them as a cache hit.
81:  try {
82:    await writeFile(path, "");
83:  } catch (err) {
84:    logger.error(
85:      `Could not remove OR truncate a rejected non-model payload "${path}"; a retry may re-serve ` +
86:        `or resume it. Delete this file manually.`,
87:      { url: logUrl, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) },
88:    );
89:  }
90:}
91:
92:/** On-disk size, or undefined when it can't be determined (fs layer stubbed in
93: *  tests, or a stat hiccup) — callers must not block a download over a missing
94: *  number, only over a number that proves corruption. */
95:async function fileSizeOrUndefined(path: string): Promise<number | undefined> {
96:  try {
97:    const st = await stat(path);
98:    return typeof st?.size === "number" ? st.size : undefined;
99:  } catch {
100:    return undefined;
101:  }
102:}
103:
104:/** Destination extensions whose bytes MUST be a binary model payload — never an
105: *  HTML page or a JSON document. A download that lands under one of these but whose
106: *  body is actually an auth-challenge / error page is the #473 false-success class:
107: *  a login HTML or `{"error":...}` JSON saved as `.safetensors`, reported as a green
108: *  success, then failing at load time ("header too large"). Extensions NOT in this
109: *  set (e.g. `.json`, `.yaml`, `.txt`, config sidecars) are left unvalidated so a
110: *  legitimate text/JSON download is never rejected. */
111:// Superset of every BINARY model format the repo recognizes as a downloadable
112:// weight file — kept in sync with the recognizers in missing-models.ts (MODEL_EXTS)
113:// and workflow-converter.ts (the model-file regex). Intentionally EXCLUDES the
114:// textual formats those recognizers also allow (`.yaml`, `.json`): those legitimately
115:// contain text, so validating them as binary would reject a valid download.
116:const MODEL_BINARY_EXTS = new Set([
117:  ".safetensors",
118:  ".safetensor",
119:  ".sft",
120:  ".ckpt",
121:  ".pt",
122:  ".pt2",
123:  ".pth",
124:  ".bin",
125:  ".gguf",
126:  ".onnx",
127:  ".vae",
128:  ".pkl",
129:  ".npz",
130:  ".msgpack",
131:]);
132:
133:/** How many leading bytes to sniff for a payload's shape. Enough to see an HTML
134: *  doctype/tag or a JSON error envelope's opening structure without reading a
135: *  multi-GB model in full. */
136:const PAYLOAD_SNIFF_BYTES = 512;
137:
138:/** Read the first `n` bytes of a file WITHOUT loading the whole thing (models are
139: *  multi-GB) — used to sniff a completed download's shape. Best-effort: returns
140: *  undefined if the file can't be opened/read (never blocks a download over a read
141: *  hiccup — the size gate has already run). */
142:async function readHead(path: string, n = PAYLOAD_SNIFF_BYTES): Promise<Buffer | undefined> {
143:  let fh: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof open>> | undefined;
144:  try {
145:    fh = await open(path, "r");
146:    const buf = Buffer.alloc(n);
147:    // Loop: a single read() can return fewer bytes than requested even when more
148:    // are available; fill up to n (or EOF) so a short first read can't truncate the
149:    // sniff window and hide an HTML/JSON body after byte ~1.
150:    let off = 0;
151:    while (off < n) {
152:      const { bytesRead } = await fh.read(buf, off, n - off, off);
153:      if (bytesRead === 0) break; // EOF
154:      off += bytesRead;
155:    }
156:    return buf.subarray(0, off);
157:  } catch {
158:    return undefined;
159:  } finally {
160:    await fh?.close().catch(() => undefined);
161:  }
162:}
163:
164:/** True when a run of bytes reads as a valid UTF-8 TEXT document (what an HTML page
165: *  or JSON error IS) rather than binary model bytes. This is a real UTF-8 validation,
166: *  not "any high byte counts as text": each non-ASCII byte must be a well-formed
167: *  UTF-8 lead followed by valid continuation bytes, and NUL / C0 control bytes
168: *  (except the text whitespace controls TAB/LF/VT/FF/CR) hard-fail. Random binary that happens to start with `<`/`{`/`[`
169: *  almost immediately hits an invalid UTF-8 lead byte (0x80–0xC1 and 0xF5–0xFF — ~26%
170: *  of all byte values) or a control byte, so it is correctly kept binary; a safetensors
171: *  whose LE header length is 60 (0x3C='<') is followed by NUL padding and fails at once.
172: *  A multibyte sequence truncated by the end of the sniff window is accepted (we only
173: *  sampled a prefix), never treated as invalid. */
174:function looksLikeText(slice: Buffer): boolean {
175:  const n = slice.length;
176:  if (n === 0) return false;
177:  let i = 0;
178:  while (i < n) {
179:    const b = slice[i];
180:    if (b < 0x80) {
181:      // ASCII: printable + the standard text whitespace controls TAB(0x09) LF(0x0a)
182:      // VT(0x0b) FF(0x0c) CR(0x0d). A form-feed / vertical-tab is valid HTML/XML
183:      // whitespace, so a real login page like `<html>\f<body>…` MUST still read as
184:      // text (else it slips through as "binary" and finalizes as a model — #473
185:      // P0-1). Any OTHER C0 control (NUL etc.) ⇒ not text.
186:      if ((b >= 0x09 && b <= 0x0d) || (b >= 0x20 && b <= 0x7e)) {
187:        i += 1;
188:        continue;
189:      }
190:      return false;
191:    }
192:    // Multibyte lead byte → determine the sequence length; invalid leads reject.
193:    let len: number;
194:    if (b >= 0xc2 && b <= 0xdf) len = 2;
195:    else if (b >= 0xe0 && b <= 0xef) len = 3;
196:    else if (b >= 0xf0 && b <= 0xf4) len = 4;
197:    else return false; // 0x80–0xC1 (bare continuation / overlong) or 0xF5–0xFF
198:    if (i + len > n) return true; // sequence truncated by the sniff window — accept
199:    // Strict continuation ranges reject overlong / surrogate / out-of-range forms:
200:    //   E0 → A0..BF (else overlong), ED → 80..9F (else surrogate),
201:    //   F0 → 90..BF (else overlong), F4 → 80..8F (else > U+10FFFF).
202:    let lo = 0x80;
203:    let hi = 0xbf;
204:    if (b === 0xe0) lo = 0xa0;
205:    else if (b === 0xed) hi = 0x9f;
206:    else if (b === 0xf0) lo = 0x90;
207:    else if (b === 0xf4) hi = 0x8f;
208:    if (slice[i + 1] < lo || slice[i + 1] > hi) return false;
209:    for (let k = 2; k < len; k += 1) {
210:      const cont = slice[i + k];
211:      if (cont < 0x80 || cont > 0xbf) return false; // not a valid continuation byte
212:    }
213:    i += len;
214:  }
215:  return true;
216:}
217:
218:/** Classify a sniffed payload head as an HTML page, a JSON document, or opaque
219: *  binary. Skips a UTF-8 BOM and leading ASCII whitespace, then requires the run
220: *  that follows to read as TEXT before declaring html/json — so a binary model that
221: *  merely starts with byte `<`/`{`/`[` (e.g. a safetensors whose LE header length is
222: *  60=0x3C or 91=0x5B, followed by NUL padding) is correctly kept as "binary":
223: *   - leading `<` + text ⇒ "html"
224: *   - leading `{`/`[` + text ⇒ "json"
225: *   - anything else ⇒ "binary" (a real model payload). */
226:/** Classify an already-DECODED text string (used for UTF-16 bodies): skips leading
227: *  whitespace, then a `<` ⇒ html / `{`|`[` ⇒ json when the run that follows is clean
228: *  text (no NUL / C0 control). A binary payload that merely decoded to a leading
229: *  `<`/`{` (e.g. a safetensors whose UTF-16 decode yields `<` then U+0000) fails the
230: *  text check and stays binary. */
231:function classifyDecodedText(s: string): "html" | "json" | "binary" {
232:  let j = 0;
233:  while (j < s.length && (s[j] === " " || s[j] === "\t" || s[j] === "\n" || s[j] === "\r")) j += 1;
234:  if (j >= s.length) return "binary";
235:  const ch = s[j];
236:  // Require the WHOLE decoded run (not just the first 64 chars) to be clean text
237:  // before declaring html/json: a binary payload that merely decoded to a leading
238:  // `<`/`{` but then hits control/replacement chars must stay binary (#473 P0-2).
239:  const rest = s.slice(j);
240:  const isText = (() => {
241:    for (const c of rest) {
242:      const cp = c.codePointAt(0)!;
243:      if (cp >= 0x09 && cp <= 0x0d) continue; // TAB/LF/VT/FF/CR — text whitespace
244:      if (cp < 0x20 || cp === 0xfffd) return false; // NUL/C0 control or replacement char
245:    }
246:    return true;
247:  })();
248:  if (ch === "<") return isText ? "html" : "binary";
249:  if (ch === "{" || ch === "[") return isText ? "json" : "binary";
250:  return "binary";
251:}
252:
253:function classifyPayload(buf: Buffer): "html" | "json" | "binary" {
254:  // A UTF-16 BOM (FF FE = LE, FE FF = BE) marks a text document — an HTML/JSON auth
255:  // page can be served UTF-16 (e.g. `text/html; charset=utf-16`, bytes FF FE 3C 00).
256:  // Decode and classify as text so it isn't mistaken for binary. A binary model that
257:  // coincidentally starts with a BOM byte pair decodes to control chars → stays binary.
258:  if (
259:    buf.length >= 2 &&
260:    ((buf[0] === 0xff && buf[1] === 0xfe) || (buf[0] === 0xfe && buf[1] === 0xff))
261:  ) {
262:    try {
263:      const label = buf[0] === 0xff ? "utf-16le" : "utf-16be";
264:      return classifyDecodedText(new TextDecoder(label).decode(buf));
265:    } catch {
266:      return "binary";
267:    }
268:  }
269:  let i = 0;
270:  if (buf.length >= 3 && buf[0] === 0xef && buf[1] === 0xbb && buf[2] === 0xbf) i = 3; // UTF-8 BOM
271:  while (
272:    i < buf.length &&
273:    (buf[i] === 0x20 || buf[i] === 0x09 || buf[i] === 0x0a || buf[i] === 0x0d)
274:  ) {
275:    i += 1;
276:  }
277:  if (i >= buf.length) return "binary";
278:  const c = buf[i];
279:  // Require the WHOLE sniffed run — the entire remaining head, not just the first 64
280:  // bytes — to read as valid text before declaring html/json. A raw binary whose head
281:  // merely STARTS with `<`/`{`/`[` but turns to non-text bytes further in (e.g. a .bin
282:  // that begins `<AAA…` then has NUL/high bytes) must be ACCEPTED as a model, not
283:  // rejected on a one-byte lead over a tiny window (#473 P0-2). A genuine HTML/JSON
284:  // auth page is text throughout the sniff window, so it still classifies correctly.
285:  const rest = buf.subarray(i);
286:  if (c === 0x3c) return looksLikeText(rest) ? "html" : "binary"; // '<'
287:  if (c === 0x7b || c === 0x5b) return looksLikeText(rest) ? "json" : "binary"; // '{' or '['
288:  return "binary";
289:}
290:
291:/** Given a completed download's sniffed head + Content-Type, decide whether the
292: *  bytes are an auth/error page masquerading as a model file (#473). Returns the
293: *  offending kind, or null when the payload is an acceptable model binary (or the
294: *  destination isn't a model-binary extension, so this validation doesn't apply).
295: *
296: *  Two independent signals, EITHER of which rejects:
297: *   1. Body magic — an HTML page's leading `<` (over a whole-window text run) and a
298: *      JSON error's leading `{`/`[` are present regardless of how the server labels
299: *      the response, so body magic catches an auth/error page even when mislabeled
300: *      `application/octet-stream`. (fetch() transparently decompresses gzip/br, so a
301: *      compressed error page still sniffs as its decoded HTML/JSON here.)
302: *   2. Content-Type — a `text/html`/`application/xhtml` or `application/json` label
303: *      on a binary-model destination is itself proof of an auth/error document, so it
304: *      rejects EVEN when the body failed to sniff as text (a stray control byte, an
305: *      exotic charset, or a truncating proxy — #473 P0-1). Real model hosts never
306: *      label a weight file text/html or application/json, so this can't reject a
307: *      legitimate binary (whose Content-Type is octet-stream or similar). */
308:function detectNonModelPayload(
309:  head: Buffer | undefined,
310:  contentType: string,
311:  modelExt: string,
312:): "html" | "json" | null {
313:  if (!modelExt || !MODEL_BINARY_EXTS.has(modelExt.toLowerCase())) return null;
314:  const kind = head ? classifyPayload(head) : "binary";
315:  if (kind === "html") return "html";
316:  if (kind === "json") return "json";
317:  // Content-Type is AUTHORITATIVE for the two textual auth/error shapes. A server
318:  // that LABELS a response `text/html`/`application/xhtml` or `application/json` for
319:  // a binary-model destination is serving a login/auth or API-error document, never
320:  // a weight file — so reject it EVEN when the body itself failed to sniff as text
321:  // (a stray control byte, an exotic charset, or a truncating proxy could otherwise
322:  // let a genuine HTML/JSON page slip through as "binary" — #473 P0-1). Real model
323:  // hosts serve weights as application/octet-stream (or similar), never as text/html
324:  // or application/json, so this can't reject a legitimate binary. (The body-magic
325:  // checks above already caught the common octet-stream-mislabeled auth page.)
326:  if (/text\/html|application\/xhtml/i.test(contentType)) return "html";
327:  if (/(^|[/+])json\b/i.test(contentType)) return "json";
328:  return null;
329:}

331:/** Actionable error for a rejected auth/error payload (#473). Names the concrete
332: *  failure (an HTML page / JSON error saved as a model) and, for CivitAI, the exact
333: *  fix (set/refresh the API token) — because a 200-with-auth-challenge is precisely
334: *  what CivitAI returns for a missing/invalid key or a gated model, and Manager /
335: *  a bare fetch would otherwise save that page under the `.safetensors` name. */
336:function nonModelPayloadError(
337:  kind: "html" | "json",
338:  url: string,
339:  logUrl: string,
340:): ModelError {
341:  let host = "";
342:  try {
343:    host = new URL(url).hostname;
344:  } catch {
345:    /* logUrl is used for reporting; host is only for the CivitAI hint */
346:  }
347:  const isCivitai = /(^|\.)civitai\.com$/i.test(host);
348:  const what =
349:    kind === "html"
350:      ? "an HTML page (an authentication/login or error page)"
351:      : "a JSON document (an API error or auth challenge)";
352:  const civitaiHint = isCivitai
353:    ? " CivitAI returns an auth challenge like this when no valid token is sent — set or refresh " +
354:      "CIVITAI_API_TOKEN (panel Settings › “Set CivitAI token…”, or the env var; create one at " +
355:      "civitai.com/user/account) and retry. Note: on a REMOTE ComfyUI target the token is NOT " +
356:      "forwarded to ComfyUI-Manager, so a locally-set token does not fix a remote Manager download."
357:    : "";
358:  return new ModelError(
359:    `Download rejected: the server returned ${what}, not a model file, but the destination is a ` +
360:      `binary model file. Refusing to save it as a model (it would land as a corrupt file under a ` +

32:const DEFAULT_CACHE_DIR = join(homedir(), ".comfyui-mcp", "cache");

37:/** Best-effort remove — never throws (rm may be stubbed in tests, or the file
38: *  may already be gone). Used on the integrity-failure cleanup paths. */
39:async function safeRm(path: string): Promise<void> {
40:  try {
41:    await rm(path, { force: true });
42:  } catch {
43:    /* best effort */
44:  }
45:}
46:
47:/** Remove `path`, returning true iff it is confirmed gone. Unlike `safeRm` this
48: *  LOGS a removal failure (EPERM/EACCES/…) instead of swallowing it silently — a
49: *  left-behind rejected artifact (a poisoned partial / cache entry) must at least be
50: *  visible, since a later cache-hit or resume could otherwise re-hit it (#473 P1). */
51:async function rmOrLog(path: string, logUrl: string): Promise<boolean> {
52:  try {
53:    await rm(path, { force: true });
54:    return true;
55:  } catch (err) {
56:    logger.warn(
57:      `Failed to remove a rejected download artifact "${path}" — a retry may re-hit it; ` +
58:        `delete it manually if the same rejection repeats.`,
59:      { url: logUrl, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) },
60:    );
61:    return false;
62:  }
63:}
64:
65:/** Discard a payload that was REJECTED as a non-model (HTML/JSON auth/error) body.
66: *  Removes the file and its resume sidecar and, CRUCIALLY, if the file itself can't
67: *  be removed, NEUTRALIZES it by truncating to 0 bytes so a retry can neither resume
68: *  onto the poisoned prefix (a 0-byte partial is treated as fresh) nor serve it as a
69: *  cache hit (a 0-byte cache entry is treated as a miss). Surfaces a hard failure via
70: *  the log when it can neither remove nor truncate (#473 P1). */
71:async function discardRejectedPayload(
72:  path: string,
73:  sidecarPath: string | undefined,
74:  logUrl: string,
75:): Promise<void> {
76:  const removed = await rmOrLog(path, logUrl);
77:  if (sidecarPath) await rmOrLog(sidecarPath, logUrl);
78:  if (removed) return;
79:  // Removal failed — neutralize the still-present poisoned bytes so a retry can't
80:  // resume onto them or re-serve them as a cache hit.
81:  try {
82:    await writeFile(path, "");
83:  } catch (err) {
84:    logger.error(
85:      `Could not remove OR truncate a rejected non-model payload "${path}"; a retry may re-serve ` +
86:        `or resume it. Delete this file manually.`,
87:      { url: logUrl, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) },
88:    );
89:  }
90:}

94: *  number, only over a number that proves corruption. */
95:async function fileSizeOrUndefined(path: string): Promise<number | undefined> {
96:  try {
97:    const st = await stat(path);
98:    return typeof st?.size === "number" ? st.size : undefined;
99:  } catch {
100:    return undefined;
101:  }
102:}

175:  const n = slice.length;
176:  if (n === 0) return false;
177:  let i = 0;
178:  while (i < n) {
179:    const b = slice[i];
180:    if (b < 0x80) {
181:      // ASCII: printable + the standard text whitespace controls TAB(0x09) LF(0x0a)

187:        i += 1;
188:        continue;
189:      }
190:      return false;
191:    }
192:    // Multibyte lead byte → determine the sequence length; invalid leads reject.
193:    let len: number;
194:    if (b >= 0xc2 && b <= 0xdf) len = 2;
195:    else if (b >= 0xe0 && b <= 0xef) len = 3;
196:    else if (b >= 0xf0 && b <= 0xf4) len = 4;
197:    else return false; // 0x80–0xC1 (bare continuation / overlong) or 0xF5–0xFF
198:    if (i + len > n) return true; // sequence truncated by the sniff window — accept
199:    // Strict continuation ranges reject overlong / surrogate / out-of-range forms:
200:    //   E0 → A0..BF (else overlong), ED → 80..9F (else surrogate),
201:    //   F0 → 90..BF (else overlong), F4 → 80..8F (else > U+10FFFF).
202:    let lo = 0x80;
203:    let hi = 0xbf;
204:    if (b === 0xe0) lo = 0xa0;
205:    else if (b === 0xed) hi = 0x9f;
206:    else if (b === 0xf0) lo = 0x90;
207:    else if (b === 0xf4) hi = 0x8f;
208:    if (slice[i + 1] < lo || slice[i + 1] > hi) return false;
209:    for (let k = 2; k < len; k += 1) {
210:      const cont = slice[i + k];
211:      if (cont < 0x80 || cont > 0xbf) return false; // not a valid continuation byte
212:    }
213:    i += len;
214:  }
215:  return true;
216:}
217:
218:/** Classify a sniffed payload head as an HTML page, a JSON document, or opaque
219: *  binary. Skips a UTF-8 BOM and leading ASCII whitespace, then requires the run
220: *  that follows to read as TEXT before declaring html/json — so a binary model that
221: *  merely starts with byte `<`/`{`/`[` (e.g. a safetensors whose LE header length is
222: *  60=0x3C or 91=0x5B, followed by NUL padding) is correctly kept as "binary":
223: *   - leading `<` + text ⇒ "html"
224: *   - leading `{`/`[` + text ⇒ "json"
225: *   - anything else ⇒ "binary" (a real model payload). */
226:/** Classify an already-DECODED text string (used for UTF-16 bodies): skips leading
227: *  whitespace, then a `<` ⇒ html / `{`|`[` ⇒ json when the run that follows is clean
228: *  text (no NUL / C0 control). A binary payload that merely decoded to a leading
229: *  `<`/`{` (e.g. a safetensors whose UTF-16 decode yields `<` then U+0000) fails the
230: *  text check and stays binary. */
231:function classifyDecodedText(s: string): "html" | "json" | "binary" {
232:  let j = 0;
233:  while (j < s.length && (s[j] === " " || s[j] === "\t" || s[j] === "\n" || s[j] === "\r")) j += 1;
234:  if (j >= s.length) return "binary";
235:  const ch = s[j];
236:  // Require the WHOLE decoded run (not just the first 64 chars) to be clean text
237:  // before declaring html/json: a binary payload that merely decoded to a leading
238:  // `<`/`{` but then hits control/replacement chars must stay binary (#473 P0-2).
239:  const rest = s.slice(j);
240:  const isText = (() => {
241:    for (const c of rest) {
242:      const cp = c.codePointAt(0)!;
243:      if (cp >= 0x09 && cp <= 0x0d) continue; // TAB/LF/VT/FF/CR — text whitespace
244:      if (cp < 0x20 || cp === 0xfffd) return false; // NUL/C0 control or replacement char
245:    }
246:    return true;
247:  })();
248:  if (ch === "<") return isText ? "html" : "binary";
249:  if (ch === "{" || ch === "[") return isText ? "json" : "binary";
250:  return "binary";
251:}
252:
253:function classifyPayload(buf: Buffer): "html" | "json" | "binary" {
254:  // A UTF-16 BOM (FF FE = LE, FE FF = BE) marks a text document — an HTML/JSON auth
255:  // page can be served UTF-16 (e.g. `text/html; charset=utf-16`, bytes FF FE 3C 00).
256:  // Decode and classify as text so it isn't mistaken for binary. A binary model that
257:  // coincidentally starts with a BOM byte pair decodes to control chars → stays binary.
258:  if (
259:    buf.length >= 2 &&
260:    ((buf[0] === 0xff && buf[1] === 0xfe) || (buf[0] === 0xfe && buf[1] === 0xff))
261:  ) {
262:    try {
263:      const label = buf[0] === 0xff ? "utf-16le" : "utf-16be";
264:      return classifyDecodedText(new TextDecoder(label).decode(buf));
265:    } catch {
266:      return "binary";
267:    }
268:  }
269:  let i = 0;
270:  if (buf.length >= 3 && buf[0] === 0xef && buf[1] === 0xbb && buf[2] === 0xbf) i = 3; // UTF-8 BOM
271:  while (
272:    i < buf.length &&
273:    (buf[i] === 0x20 || buf[i] === 0x09 || buf[i] === 0x0a || buf[i] === 0x0d)
274:  ) {
275:    i += 1;
276:  }
277:  if (i >= buf.length) return "binary";
278:  const c = buf[i];
279:  // Require the WHOLE sniffed run — the entire remaining head, not just the first 64
280:  // bytes — to read as valid text before declaring html/json. A raw binary whose head
281:  // merely STARTS with `<`/`{`/`[` but turns to non-text bytes further in (e.g. a .bin
282:  // that begins `<AAA…` then has NUL/high bytes) must be ACCEPTED as a model, not
283:  // rejected on a one-byte lead over a tiny window (#473 P0-2). A genuine HTML/JSON
284:  // auth page is text throughout the sniff window, so it still classifies correctly.
285:  const rest = buf.subarray(i);
286:  if (c === 0x3c) return looksLikeText(rest) ? "html" : "binary"; // '<'
287:  if (c === 0x7b || c === 0x5b) return looksLikeText(rest) ? "json" : "binary"; // '{' or '['
288:  return "binary";
289:}
290:
291:/** Given a completed download's sniffed head + Content-Type, decide whether the
292: *  bytes are an auth/error page masquerading as a model file (#473). Returns the
293: *  offending kind, or null when the payload is an acceptable model binary (or the
294: *  destination isn't a model-binary extension, so this validation doesn't apply).
295: *
296: *  Two independent signals, EITHER of which rejects:
297: *   1. Body magic — an HTML page's leading `<` (over a whole-window text run) and a
298: *      JSON error's leading `{`/`[` are present regardless of how the server labels
299: *      the response, so body magic catches an auth/error page even when mislabeled
300: *      `application/octet-stream`. (fetch() transparently decompresses gzip/br, so a
301: *      compressed error page still sniffs as its decoded HTML/JSON here.)
302: *   2. Content-Type — a `text/html`/`application/xhtml` or `application/json` label
303: *      on a binary-model destination is itself proof of an auth/error document, so it
304: *      rejects EVEN when the body failed to sniff as text (a stray control byte, an
305: *      exotic charset, or a truncating proxy — #473 P0-1). Real model hosts never
306: *      label a weight file text/html or application/json, so this can't reject a
307: *      legitimate binary (whose Content-Type is octet-stream or similar). */
308:function detectNonModelPayload(
309:  head: Buffer | undefined,
310:  contentType: string,
311:  modelExt: string,
312:): "html" | "json" | null {
313:  if (!modelExt || !MODEL_BINARY_EXTS.has(modelExt.toLowerCase())) return null;
314:  const kind = head ? classifyPayload(head) : "binary";
315:  if (kind === "html") return "html";
316:  if (kind === "json") return "json";
317:  // Content-Type is AUTHORITATIVE for the two textual auth/error shapes. A server
318:  // that LABELS a response `text/html`/`application/xhtml` or `application/json` for
319:  // a binary-model destination is serving a login/auth or API-error document, never
320:  // a weight file — so reject it EVEN when the body itself failed to sniff as text
321:  // (a stray control byte, an exotic charset, or a truncating proxy could otherwise
322:  // let a genuine HTML/JSON page slip through as "binary" — #473 P0-1). Real model
323:  // hosts serve weights as application/octet-stream (or similar), never as text/html
324:  // or application/json, so this can't reject a legitimate binary. (The body-magic
325:  // checks above already caught the common octet-stream-mislabeled auth page.)
326:  if (/text\/html|application\/xhtml/i.test(contentType)) return "html";
327:  if (/(^|[/+])json\b/i.test(contentType)) return "json";
328:  return null;
329:}
330:
331:/** Actionable error for a rejected auth/error payload (#473). Names the concrete
332: *  failure (an HTML page / JSON error saved as a model) and, for CivitAI, the exact
333: *  fix (set/refresh the API token) — because a 200-with-auth-challenge is precisely
334: *  what CivitAI returns for a missing/invalid key or a gated model, and Manager /
335: *  a bare fetch would otherwise save that page under the `.safetensors` name. */
336:function nonModelPayloadError(
337:  kind: "html" | "json",
338:  url: string,
339:  logUrl: string,
340:): ModelError {
341:  let host = "";
342:  try {
343:    host = new URL(url).hostname;
344:  } catch {
345:    /* logUrl is used for reporting; host is only for the CivitAI hint */
346:  }
347:  const isCivitai = /(^|\.)civitai\.com$/i.test(host);
348:  const what =
349:    kind === "html"
350:      ? "an HTML page (an authentication/login or error page)"
351:      : "a JSON document (an API error or auth challenge)";
352:  const civitaiHint = isCivitai
353:    ? " CivitAI returns an auth challenge like this when no valid token is sent — set or refresh " +
354:      "CIVITAI_API_TOKEN (panel Settings › “Set CivitAI token…”, or the env var; create one at " +
355:      "civitai.com/user/account) and retry. Note: on a REMOTE ComfyUI target the token is NOT " +
356:      "forwarded to ComfyUI-Manager, so a locally-set token does not fix a remote Manager download."
357:    : "";
358:  return new ModelError(
359:    `Download rejected: the server returned ${what}, not a model file, but the destination is a ` +
360:      `binary model file. Refusing to save it as a model (it would land as a corrupt file under a ` +

exec
"C:\\Windows\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command 'rg -n "''^" src/services/download-cache.ts | Select-Object -Skip 360 -First 420' in C:\Users\Artokun\code\comfyui-mcp\.claude\worktrees\agent-a357b101a6eb58cf0
2026-07-30T20:06:47.998452Z ERROR codex_core::tools::router: error=Exit code: 1
Wall time: 0.2 seconds
Output:
361:      `false success and fail at load time, e.g. "header too large").${civitaiHint}`,
362:    { url: logUrl },
363:  );
364:}
365:
366:/** Validate that a COMPLETED file at `targetPath` is a real model payload — not an
367: *  HTML/JSON auth/error page — when the destination is a binary-model extension
368: *  (#473). FAILS CLOSED: an offending payload OR an unreadable-for-sniffing file (we
369: *  can't confirm it) throws, after `onReject` cleans up, so a corrupt file is never
370: *  finalized under a success. A no-op for non-model destinations. `contentType` is a
371: *  secondary signal (empty on cache-hit/cloud paths, where only the bytes are known). */
372:async function assertModelPayloadOrThrow(opts: {
373:  targetPath: string;
374:  modelExt: string;
375:  contentType: string;
376:  url: string;
377:  logUrl: string;
378:  onReject?: () => Promise<void>;
379:  /** Defaults to TRUE for every finalize path (HTTP, cache-hit, cloud S3/Azure): an
380:   *  unreadable/empty sniff head leaves the payload UNVERIFIED and is REJECTED —
381:   *  cannot verify ⇒ do not finalize (#473 P0-3). (A genuinely 0-byte download is
382:   *  already rejected upstream by assertComplete / the cache-hit 0-byte guard, so
383:   *  fail-closed here only trips on a nonzero file we couldn't read.) The flag is kept
384:   *  as a seam for callers/tests that need best-effort behavior, but no production
385:   *  path passes false. */
386:  failClosed?: boolean;
387:}): Promise<void> {
388:  const { targetPath, modelExt, contentType, url, logUrl, failClosed = true } = opts;
389:  if (!modelExt || !MODEL_BINARY_EXTS.has(modelExt.toLowerCase())) return;
390:  const head = await readHead(targetPath);
391:  // An unreadable OR unexpectedly-empty head (a non-empty file that read 0 bytes
392:  // under an fs race) leaves the payload UNVERIFIED. On the fail-closed paths refuse
393:  // to finalize it rather than trust it (#473/#467); on the best-effort cloud path,
394:  // skip. (A genuinely 0-byte download is already rejected upstream by assertComplete
395:  // / the cache-hit 0-byte guard.)
396:  if (head === undefined || head.length === 0) {
397:    if (!failClosed) return;
398:    await opts.onReject?.();
399:    throw new ModelError(
400:      `Download could not be verified: the completed file could not be read to confirm it is a ` +
401:        `model payload (and not an HTML/JSON auth/error page). Not finalizing it as a model; retry.`,
402:      { url: logUrl },
403:    );
404:  }
405:  const kind = detectNonModelPayload(head, contentType, modelExt);
406:  if (!kind) return;
407:  await opts.onReject?.();
408:  throw nonModelPayloadError(kind, url, logUrl);
409:}
410:
411:/** POSITIVELY true only when `path` is confirmed discarded — it no longer exists
412: *  (stat throws ENOENT) or exists but is empty (size 0). A non-ENOENT stat error
413: *  (a transient fs hiccup) or a still-present non-empty file returns false, so a
414: *  swallowed removal failure is never mistaken for a completed discard (#467). */
415:async function partialConfirmedDiscarded(path: string): Promise<boolean> {
416:  try {
417:    const st = await stat(path);
418:    return typeof st?.size === "number" && st.size === 0;
419:  } catch (err) {
420:    return (err as NodeJS.ErrnoException)?.code === "ENOENT";
421:  }
422:}
423:
424:/**
425: * Extract the useful diagnostic bits from an error thrown by `fetch()` itself
426: * (a network-layer failure, not an HTTP status). undici surfaces these as
427: * `TypeError: fetch failed` whose real reason lives on `.cause` (an Error with
428: * a `code`/`errno` such as ENOTFOUND, ECONNRESET, UND_ERR_CONNECT_TIMEOUT, or
429: * a TLS `ERR_TLS_CERT_ALTNAME_INVALID`). The top-level message is the useless
430: * generic "fetch failed" (issue #411) — the actionable detail is one level
431: * down, so unwrap the cause chain into a single readable string.
432: */
433:function describeFetchError(err: unknown): { message: string; code?: string } {
434:  const parts: string[] = [];
435:  let code: string | undefined;
436:  let cur: unknown = err;
437:  const seen = new Set<unknown>();
438:  while (cur instanceof Error && !seen.has(cur)) {
439:    seen.add(cur);
440:    const c = (cur as { code?: unknown }).code;
441:    if (typeof c === "string" && !code) code = c;
442:    const label = typeof c === "string" ? `${cur.message} (${c})` : cur.message;
443:    if (label && !parts.includes(label)) parts.push(label);
444:    cur = (cur as { cause?: unknown }).cause;
445:  }
446:  if (parts.length === 0) parts.push(String(err));
447:  return { message: parts.join(": "), code };
448:}
449:
450:/**
451: * Fetch that converts a network-layer failure into a ModelError carrying the
452: * unwrapped `cause` — so a Hugging Face Xet / CAS host that fails DNS, TLS, a
453: * connect timeout, or a proxy reset reports WHY instead of a bare "fetch
454: * failed" (issue #411). HTTP responses (any status) pass straight through; only
455: * a thrown fetch is wrapped. The ModelError is rethrown as-is by downloadWithCache
456: * (it never masks a ModelError), so the actionable cause reaches the tool result.
457: */
458:async function fetchOrThrow(
459:  url: string,
460:  init: RequestInit,
461:  logUrl: string,
462:): Promise<Response> {
463:  try {
464:    return await fetch(url, init);
465:  } catch (err) {
466:    const { message, code } = describeFetchError(err);
467:    throw new ModelError(
468:      `Download request to the model host failed at the network layer: ${message}. ` +
469:        `This is a connectivity/TLS/proxy failure reaching the file host (for Hugging Face ` +
470:        `this is often the Xet/CAS CDN, e.g. cas-bridge.xethub.hf.co) — not an HTTP error. ` +
471:        `Check DNS/connectivity to the host, any corporate proxy or firewall, and system time/TLS certs; ` +
472:        `set HF_ENDPOINT to a reachable mirror if huggingface.co is blocked in your region, then retry.`,
473:      { url: logUrl, code, cause: message },
474:    );
475:  }
476:}

478:export const downloadCacheFs = {
479:  copyFile,
480:  link,
481:  mkdir,
482:  readdir,
483:  rename,
484:  rm,
485:  stat,
486:  utimes,
487:};
