489:/** Identifies a download for the panel progress tray (id = stable key, name =
490: *  the friendly file name). Absent for internal/cache-only callers. */
491:export interface ProgressMeta {
492:  id: string;
493:  name: string;
494:}
495:
496:export interface DownloadCacheOptions {
497:  url: string;
498:  headers: Record<string, string>;
499:  targetPath: string;
500:  logUrl?: string;
501:  storageAuth?: CloudStorageAuth;
502:  progress?: ProgressMeta;
503:  /** Sink for this download's resume decision, reported to the CALLER (the job)
504:   *  so it stores the outcome on itself — never in a shared keyed map that could
505:   *  misattribute it to another job (#467). Not called on a coalesced/cache-hit
506:   *  path (no physical resume happened). */
507:  onResume?: ResumeReporter;
508:}
509:
510:export interface DownloadCacheResult {
511:  targetPath: string;
512:  usedCache: boolean;
513:  cachePath?: string;
514:  materializedBy?: "hardlink" | "copy";
515:}
516:
517:function cacheDir(): string {
518:  return resolve(process.env.COMFYUI_DOWNLOAD_CACHE_DIR || DEFAULT_CACHE_DIR);
519:}
520:
521:function cacheSizeLimitBytes(): number {
522:  const raw = Number(process.env.COMFYUI_LRU_CACHE_SIZE_GB ?? "0");
523:  if (!Number.isFinite(raw) || raw <= 0) return 0;
524:  return raw * 1024 * 1024 * 1024;
525:}
526:
527:/** Representation-affecting request headers, folded into the cache identity so a
528: *  same-URL fetch carrying DIFFERENT auth/headers (two users' Bearer tokens, a
529: *  Cookie/API-key, or ANY custom header like `X-Custom-Auth` that selects a
530: *  user-scoped or gated representation) can NEVER share a cache entry / in-flight
531: *  stream and install the wrong bytes (#467 P1-2). Query-param auth already
532: *  varies the URL itself (applyDownloadAuth folds it in), so this covers the
533: *  header case the URL can't. Hashes EVERY caller-supplied header (an allowlist
534: *  would silently miss custom auth headers) — these are the request headers built
535: *  from the caller's auth/config, NOT the volatile hop headers (Range/If-Range),
536: *  which are added later inside streamUrlToFile and never reach here. Empty ⇒ no
537: *  header line is added to the identity (the URL still carries the v2 namespace). */
538:function representationKey(headers: Record<string, string>): string {
539:  const relevant = Object.keys(headers)
540:    .sort((a, b) => a.toLowerCase().localeCompare(b.toLowerCase()))
541:    .map((k) => `${k.toLowerCase()}=${headers[k]}`)
542:    .join("\n");
543:  return relevant ? createHash("sha256").update(relevant).digest("hex").slice(0, 12) : "";
544:}
545:
546:/** Cache-identity namespace. Bumped when the identity SCHEME changes so entries
547: *  from an older scheme can never be served under the new one. Critically this
548: *  fixes a cross-auth leak (#467 P1-C): the PRE-header-aware code cached
549: *  header-authenticated downloads under the BARE URL, so without a namespace a
550: *  post-upgrade UNAUTHENTICATED caller (also bare-URL) could be served bytes that
551: *  were cached earlier under someone's auth. We can't tell a legacy entry's
552: *  provenance, so ALL new identities — authed AND unauthed — are prefixed, which
553: *  orphans every legacy `sha256(url)` entry (a one-time re-download; orphans age
554: *  out via LRU when COMFYUI_LRU_CACHE_SIZE_GB is set, else are inert on disk). */
555:const CACHE_NS = "v2";
556:
557:function cacheIdentity(
558:  url: string,
559:  headers: Record<string, string>,
560:  storageAuth?: CloudStorageAuth,
561:): string {
562:  const repr = representationKey(headers);
563:  // The EFFECTIVE cloud principal (explicit storageAuth MERGED with env creds/
564:  // endpoint/region), not just explicit storageAuth — so an env-authenticated cloud
565:  // entry can't be served later under different/absent creds (#467 P1-B).
566:  const cloud = supportsCloudDownload(url) ? cloudPrincipalKey(url, storageAuth) : "";
567:  // Namespaced for BOTH authed and unauthed so neither can collide with a legacy
568:  // bare-URL entry of unknown auth provenance (#467 P1-C). Representation-affecting
569:  // HTTP headers AND cloud credentials each add a line so different auth can never
570:  // share bytes (#467 P1-2). Empty discriminators are omitted, so unauthenticated
571:  // public downloads stay stable under the v2 namespace.
572:  let id = `${CACHE_NS}\n${url}`;
573:  if (repr) id += `\n${repr}`;
574:  if (cloud) id += `\ncloud:${cloud}`;
575:  return id;
576:}
577:
578:function cachePathForUrl(
579:  url: string,
580:  headers: Record<string, string> = {},
581:  storageAuth?: CloudStorageAuth,
582:): string {
583:  const hash = createHash("sha256")
584:    .update(cacheIdentity(url, headers, storageAuth))
585:    .digest("hex")
586:    .slice(0, HASH_CHARS);
587:  let extension = "";
588:  try {
589:    extension = extname(basename(new URL(url).pathname));
590:  } catch {
591:    // Callers validate URLs before reaching this layer; keep the cache helper
592:    // defensive so fallback direct downloads can still surface the real error.
593:  }
594:  return join(cacheDir(), `${hash}${extension}`);
595:}
596:
597:async function touch(path: string): Promise<void> {
598:  const now = new Date();
599:  await downloadCacheFs.utimes(path, now, now);
600:}
601:
602:/** A parsed resume sidecar: the change-detection validator (ETag / X-Linked-Etag /
603: *  Last-Modified) and, when the first attempt knew it, the AUTHORITATIVE full-file
604: *  size. The size lets a later resume reject a 206 whose Content-Range total
605: *  DISAGREES with what the original response declared — a server that understates
606: *  the total on resume (e.g. `bytes 4-7/8` for a file first seen as 4096) can no
607: *  longer finalize a short prefix as complete (#467). */
608:interface ResumeSidecar {
609:  validator: string;
610:  total?: number;
611:}
612:
613:/** Read the resume sidecar (line 1 = validator, optional line 2 = total) or null.
614: *  Best-effort: a missing/unreadable sidecar just means "resume without an If-Range
615: *  guard" — never fatal. Backward compatible with pre-total single-line sidecars. */
616:async function readValidatorSidecar(path: string): Promise<ResumeSidecar | null> {
617:  try {
618:    const raw = await readFile(path, "utf-8");
619:    const lines = raw.split("\n");
620:    const validator = (lines[0] ?? "").trim();
621:    if (validator.length === 0) return null;
622:    const total = lines.length > 1 ? Number(lines[1].trim()) : NaN;
623:    return Number.isFinite(total) && total > 0 ? { validator, total } : { validator };
624:  } catch {
625:    return null;
626:  }
627:}
628:
629:/** Persist the resume validator (+ authoritative total when known) next to a
630: *  .partial. Best-effort — a failure here only costs the change-detection guard on
631: *  a later resume. The validator is a single-line header value; the optional total
632: *  goes on line 2. */
633:async function writeValidatorSidecar(path: string, value: string, total?: number): Promise<void> {
634:  try {
635:    const body = typeof total === "number" && total > 0 ? `${value}\n${total}` : value;
636:    await writeFile(path, body, "utf-8");
637:  } catch {
638:    /* best effort */
639:  }
640:}
641:
642:/** The strongest resume validator a response can offer, preferring Hugging
643: *  Face's content-addressed `X-Linked-Etag` (the LFS/Xet object hash — carried
644: *  on the huggingface.co/resolve 302, NOT on the CAS CDN's final 200) over a
645: *  plain ETag, falling back to Last-Modified. Capturing X-Linked-Etag from the
646: *  redirect is what lets HF Xet downloads persist a sidecar at all (#467): the
647: *  final CAS response returns neither ETag nor Last-Modified, so without this a
648: *  Xet partial could NEVER be safely resumed. */
649:function extractValidator(res: Response): string | null {
650:  return (
651:    res.headers.get("x-linked-etag") ||
652:    res.headers.get("etag") ||
653:    res.headers.get("last-modified")
654:  );
655:}
656:
657:async function streamUrlToFile(
658:  url: string,
659:  targetPath: string,
660:  headers: Record<string, string>,
661:  logUrl = redactUrlForLogs(url),
662:  storageAuth: CloudStorageAuth = {},
663:  resumeFromBytes = 0,
664:  progress?: ProgressMeta,
665:  resumable = false,
666:  /** Sink for THIS physical download's resume decision, threaded from the job so
667:   *  the outcome is stored on that job — never in a shared keyed map that could
668:   *  misattribute it to another job (#467). Absent for internal/direct callers. */
669:  onResume?: ResumeReporter,
670:  /** Extension of the FINAL destination (e.g. ".safetensors"), NOT of the .partial
671:   *  we stream into. When it's a binary-model extension the completed payload is
672:   *  validated as a real model — an HTML/JSON auth/error body is rejected rather
673:   *  than finalized as a corrupt model under a false success (#473). Empty ⇒ no
674:   *  payload validation (unknown/non-model destination). */
675:  modelExt = "",
676:): Promise<void> {
677:  if (supportsCloudDownload(url)) {
678:    // Cloud downloaders (S3/Azure) don't range-resume — they overwrite the target.
679:    // If a partial exists it's being discarded; surface that (#467) instead of a
680:    // silent restart. Truncate it OURSELVES first and CONFIRM (throw on failure)
681:    // BEFORE reporting discarded:true — the cloud SDKs validate the request/body
682:    // before opening their write stream, so reporting pre-download could otherwise
683:    // claim a discard that a later auth/network failure never performed (P1-1).
684:    if (resumable && resumeFromBytes > 0) {
685:      try {
686:        await writeFile(targetPath, "");
687:      } catch (err) {
688:        throw new ModelError(
689:          `Download restart failed: could not truncate the stale ${resumeFromBytes}-byte partial ` +
690:            `before a cloud re-download. Retry (a fresh attempt restarts from 0).`,
691:          { url: logUrl, cause: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) },
692:        );
693:      }
694:      logger.warn(
695:        `Discarded a ${resumeFromBytes}-byte partial download and restarting from 0: cloud ` +
696:          `downloads (S3/Azure) don't support byte-range resume, so the partial was overwritten — ` +
697:          `re-downloading in full.`,
698:        { url: logUrl, discardedBytes: resumeFromBytes },
699:      );
700:      onResume?.({ outcome: "declined:full-response", discardedBytes: resumeFromBytes, discarded: true });
701:    }
702:    await downloadCloudUrlToFile(url, targetPath, storageAuth);
703:    // #343 edge: the S3/Azure path bypasses the HTTP size gate below. The cloud
704:    // downloaders verify their own Content-Length (truncation), but a stream
705:    // that yielded ZERO bytes can still resolve cleanly — backstop it here so a
706:    // 0-byte cloud object can never be reported as a successful download.
707:    const actual = await fileSizeOrUndefined(targetPath);
708:    if (actual === 0) {
709:      await safeRm(targetPath);
710:      throw new ModelError(
711:        "Download produced a 0-byte file — the cloud source (S3/Azure) sent no data. Removed it; retry.",
712:        { url: logUrl },
713:      );
714:    }
715:    // A cloud (S3/Azure) object can also be an error document — e.g. an XML
716:    // AccessDenied body saved under a `.safetensors` name. No HTTP Content-Type is
717:    // available here, so sniff the body alone (an XML/HTML error starts with `<`).
718:    await assertModelPayloadOrThrow({
719:      targetPath,
720:      modelExt,
721:      contentType: "",
722:      url,
723:      logUrl,
724:      onReject: () => discardRejectedPayload(targetPath, undefined, logUrl),
725:      // FAIL CLOSED, same as the HTTP path: a cloud (S3/Azure) object can be an XML/
726:      // HTML AccessDenied body saved under a `.safetensors` name, and if we CAN'T
727:      // sniff the completed file (a transient open/read failure) we must NOT finalize
728:      // it — cannot verify ⇒ do not finalize (#473 P0-3). Previously this was a
729:      // best-effort skip, which let an unsniffable auth/error body get renamed in.
730:      failClosed: true,
731:    });
732:    return;
733:  }
734:
735:  // Resumable downloads: when a partial file exists at targetPath we ask the
736:  // server for the remaining bytes via Range. If the server returns 206 with
737:  // matching Content-Range we append; if it returns 200 (range unsupported,
738:  // or the file changed upstream) we truncate and restart. Idea from
739:  // josephoibrahim/comfy-cozy.
740:  //
741:  // #343 edge: a plain Range resume is unsafe if the upstream file CHANGED
742:  // between the two calls — we would append fresh bytes onto a stale prefix and
743:  // the size check could still pass, producing a corrupt file. Guard it with a
744:  // conditional resume: we persist the first response's validator (ETag, else
745:  // Last-Modified) in a sidecar next to the .partial and replay it as `If-Range`
746:  // on resume. Per RFC 9110 the server then returns 206 (append) ONLY if the
747:  // resource is byte-for-byte unchanged, otherwise 200 with the full body (which
748:  // our append-vs-truncate logic below restarts cleanly). Belt-and-braces, we
749:  // also validate the 206 Content-Range starts exactly at our resume offset.
750:  const validatorSidecar = `${targetPath}.etag`;
751:  let currentUrl = url;
752:  let currentHeaders = headers;
753:  // How many bytes we will actually attempt to resume from. A resume is only
754:  // SAFE when we can prove the upstream file is unchanged, which requires the
755:  // validator (ETag/Last-Modified) we persisted on the first attempt. Without a
756:  // sidecar — a stale partial from a pre-fix build, or a server that sent no
757:  // validator — we cannot detect a changed file, so we must NOT append: fall
758:  // back to a clean restart (Range omitted, the "w" flag truncates the partial).
759:  let effectiveResume = resumeFromBytes;
760:  // Did we actually ask the server to resume (Range + If-Range)? Used after the
761:  // response to distinguish a taken resume (206) from an If-Range MISS (200 =
762:  // upstream changed) so we can surface WHY the partial was discarded (#467).
763:  let requestedResume = false;
764:  // Set when a partial existed but no sidecar validator did, so we declined to
765:  // resume. The discard is only REPORTED once we actually truncate it (after a
766:  // successful response), so a fetch/redirect/status failure before then can't
767:  // falsely claim the partial was discarded (#467 codex round 4).
768:  let resumeDeclinedNoValidator = false;
769:  // The persisted content-addressed validator we are resuming AGAINST — kept so
770:  // we can re-compare it to the value the resolve redirect reports NOW, and
771:  // refuse the append ourselves if they differ (belt-and-braces on top of the
772:  // origin's If-Range: we never trust a CAS 206 whose upstream object changed).
773:  let priorValidator: string | null = null;
774:  // The authoritative full-file size the ORIGINAL response declared (persisted in
775:  // the sidecar), if known — a resume 206 whose Content-Range total DISAGREES with
776:  // this is a server understating the size, and must be refused (#467).
777:  let priorTotal: number | undefined;
778:  if (resumeFromBytes > 0) {
779:    const sidecar = resumable ? await readValidatorSidecar(validatorSidecar) : null;
780:    priorValidator = sidecar?.validator ?? null;

 exited 1 in 161ms:
361:      `false success and fail at load time, e.g. "header too large").${civitaiHint}`,
362:    { url: logUrl },
363:  );
364:}
365:
366:/** Validate that a COMPLETED file at `targetPath` is a real model payload — not an
367: *  HTML/JSON auth/error page — when the destination is a binary-model extension
368: *  (#473). FAILS CLOSED: an offending payload OR an unreadable-for-sniffing file (we
369: *  can't confirm it) throws, after `onReject` cleans up, so a corrupt file is never
370: *  finalized under a success. A no-op for non-model destinations. `contentType` is a
371: *  secondary signal (empty on cache-hit/cloud paths, where only the bytes are known). */
372:async function assertModelPayloadOrThrow(opts: {
373:  targetPath: string;
374:  modelExt: string;
375:  contentType: string;
376:  url: string;
377:  logUrl: string;
378:  onReject?: () => Promise<void>;
379:  /** Defaults to TRUE for every finalize path (HTTP, cache-hit, cloud S3/Azure): an
380:   *  unreadable/empty sniff head leaves the payload UNVERIFIED and is REJECTED —
381:   *  cannot verify ⇒ do not finalize (#473 P0-3). (A genuinely 0-byte download is
382:   *  already rejected upstream by assertComplete / the cache-hit 0-byte guard, so
383:   *  fail-closed here only trips on a nonzero file we couldn't read.) The flag is kept
384:   *  as a seam for callers/tests that need best-effort behavior, but no production
385:   *  path passes false. */
386:  failClosed?: boolean;
387:}): Promise<void> {
388:  const { targetPath, modelExt, contentType, url, logUrl, failClosed = true } = opts;
389:  if (!modelExt || !MODEL_BINARY_EXTS.has(modelExt.toLowerCase())) return;
390:  const head = await readHead(targetPath);
391:  // An unreadable OR unexpectedly-empty head (a non-empty file that read 0 bytes
392:  // under an fs race) leaves the payload UNVERIFIED. On the fail-closed paths refuse
393:  // to finalize it rather than trust it (#473/#467); on the best-effort cloud path,
394:  // skip. (A genuinely 0-byte download is already rejected upstream by assertComplete
395:  // / the cache-hit 0-byte guard.)
396:  if (head === undefined || head.length === 0) {
397:    if (!failClosed) return;
398:    await opts.onReject?.();
399:    throw new ModelError(
400:      `Download could not be verified: the completed file could not be read to confirm it is a ` +
401:        `model payload (and not an HTML/JSON auth/error page). Not finalizing it as a model; retry.`,
402:      { url: logUrl },
403:    );
404:  }
405:  const kind = detectNonModelPayload(head, contentType, modelExt);
406:  if (!kind) return;
407:  await opts.onReject?.();
408:  throw nonModelPayloadError(kind, url, logUrl);
409:}
410:
411:/** POSITIVELY true only when `path` is confirmed discarded — it no longer exists
412: *  (stat throws ENOENT) or exists but is empty (size 0). A non-ENOENT stat error
413: *  (a transient fs hiccup) or a still-present non-empty file returns false, so a
414: *  swallowed removal failure is never mistaken for a completed discard (#467). */
415:async function partialConfirmedDiscarded(path: string): Promise<boolean> {
416:  try {
417:    const st = await stat(path);
418:    return typeof st?.size === "number" && st.size === 0;
419:  } catch (err) {
420:    return (err as NodeJS.ErrnoException)?.code === "ENOENT";
421:  }
422:}
423:
424:/**
425: * Extract the useful diagnostic bits from an error thrown by `fetch()` itself
426: * (a network-layer failure, not an HTTP status). undici surfaces these as
427: * `TypeError: fetch failed` whose real reason lives on `.cause` (an Error with
428: * a `code`/`errno` such as ENOTFOUND, ECONNRESET, UND_ERR_CONNECT_TIMEOUT, or
429: * a TLS `ERR_TLS_CERT_ALTNAME_INVALID`). The top-level message is the useless
430: * generic "fetch failed" (issue #411) — the actionable detail is one level
431: * down, so unwrap the cause chain into a single readable string.
432: */
433:function describeFetchError(err: unknown): { message: string; code?: string } {
434:  const parts: string[] = [];
435:  let code: string | undefined;
436:  let cur: unknown = err;
437:  const seen = new Set<unknown>();
438:  while (cur instanceof Error && !seen.has(cur)) {
439:    seen.add(cur);
440:    const c = (cur as { code?: unknown }).code;
441:    if (typeof c === "string" && !code) code = c;
442:    const label = typeof c === "string" ? `${cur.message} (${c})` : cur.message;
443:    if (label && !parts.includes(label)) parts.push(label);
444:    cur = (cur as { cause?: unknown }).cause;
445:  }
446:  if (parts.length === 0) parts.push(String(err));
447:  return { message: parts.join(": "), code };
448:}
449:
450:/**
451: * Fetch that converts a network-layer failure into a ModelError carrying the
452: * unwrapped `cause` — so a Hugging Face Xet / CAS host that fails DNS, TLS, a
453: * connect timeout, or a proxy reset reports WHY instead of a bare "fetch
454: * failed" (issue #411). HTTP responses (any status) pass straight through; only
455: * a thrown fetch is wrapped. The ModelError is rethrown as-is by downloadWithCache
456: * (it never masks a ModelError), so the actionable cause reaches the tool result.
457: */
458:async function fetchOrThrow(
459:  url: string,
460:  init: RequestInit,
461:  logUrl: string,
462:): Promise<Response> {
463:  try {
464:    return await fetch(url, init);
465:  } catch (err) {
466:    const { message, code } = describeFetchError(err);
467:    throw new ModelError(
468:      `Download request to the model host failed at the network layer: ${message}. ` +
469:        `This is a connectivity/TLS/proxy failure reaching the file host (for Hugging Face ` +
470:        `this is often the Xet/CAS CDN, e.g. cas-bridge.xethub.hf.co) — not an HTTP error. ` +
471:        `Check DNS/connectivity to the host, any corporate proxy or firewall, and system time/TLS certs; ` +
472:        `set HF_ENDPOINT to a reachable mirror if huggingface.co is blocked in your region, then retry.`,
473:      { url: logUrl, code, cause: message },
474:    );
475:  }
476:}

478:export const downloadCacheFs = {
479:  copyFile,
480:  link,
481:  mkdir,
482:  readdir,
483:  rename,
484:  rm,
485:  stat,
486:  utimes,
487:};
488:
489:/** Identifies a download for the panel progress tray (id = stable key, name =
490: *  the friendly file name). Absent for internal/cache-only callers. */
491:export interface ProgressMeta {
492:  id: string;
493:  name: string;
494:}
495:
496:export interface DownloadCacheOptions {
497:  url: string;
498:  headers: Record<string, string>;
499:  targetPath: string;
500:  logUrl?: string;
501:  storageAuth?: CloudStorageAuth;
502:  progress?: ProgressMeta;
503:  /** Sink for this download's resume decision, reported to the CALLER (the job)
504:   *  so it stores the outcome on itself — never in a shared keyed map that could
505:   *  misattribute it to another job (#467). Not called on a coalesced/cache-hit
506:   *  path (no physical resume happened). */
507:  onResume?: ResumeReporter;
508:}
509:
510:export interface DownloadCacheResult {
511:  targetPath: string;
512:  usedCache: boolean;
513:  cachePath?: string;
514:  materializedBy?: "hardlink" | "copy";
515:}
516:
517:function cacheDir(): string {
518:  return resolve(process.env.COMFYUI_DOWNLOAD_CACHE_DIR || DEFAULT_CACHE_DIR);
519:}
520:
521:function cacheSizeLimitBytes(): number {
522:  const raw = Number(process.env.COMFYUI_LRU_CACHE_SIZE_GB ?? "0");
523:  if (!Number.isFinite(raw) || raw <= 0) return 0;
524:  return raw * 1024 * 1024 * 1024;
525:}
526:
527:/** Representation-affecting request headers, folded into the cache identity so a
528: *  same-URL fetch carrying DIFFERENT auth/headers (two users' Bearer tokens, a
529: *  Cookie/API-key, or ANY custom header like `X-Custom-Auth` that selects a
530: *  user-scoped or gated representation) can NEVER share a cache entry / in-flight
531: *  stream and install the wrong bytes (#467 P1-2). Query-param auth already
532: *  varies the URL itself (applyDownloadAuth folds it in), so this covers the
533: *  header case the URL can't. Hashes EVERY caller-supplied header (an allowlist
534: *  would silently miss custom auth headers) — these are the request headers built
535: *  from the caller's auth/config, NOT the volatile hop headers (Range/If-Range),
536: *  which are added later inside streamUrlToFile and never reach here. Empty ⇒ no
537: *  header line is added to the identity (the URL still carries the v2 namespace). */
538:function representationKey(headers: Record<string, string>): string {
539:  const relevant = Object.keys(headers)
540:    .sort((a, b) => a.toLowerCase().localeCompare(b.toLowerCase()))
541:    .map((k) => `${k.toLowerCase()}=${headers[k]}`)
542:    .join("\n");
543:  return relevant ? createHash("sha256").update(relevant).digest("hex").slice(0, 12) : "";
544:}
545:
546:/** Cache-identity namespace. Bumped when the identity SCHEME changes so entries
547: *  from an older scheme can never be served under the new one. Critically this
548: *  fixes a cross-auth leak (#467 P1-C): the PRE-header-aware code cached
549: *  header-authenticated downloads under the BARE URL, so without a namespace a
550: *  post-upgrade UNAUTHENTICATED caller (also bare-URL) could be served bytes that
551: *  were cached earlier under someone's auth. We can't tell a legacy entry's
552: *  provenance, so ALL new identities — authed AND unauthed — are prefixed, which
553: *  orphans every legacy `sha256(url)` entry (a one-time re-download; orphans age
554: *  out via LRU when COMFYUI_LRU_CACHE_SIZE_GB is set, else are inert on disk). */
555:const CACHE_NS = "v2";
556:
557:function cacheIdentity(
558:  url: string,
559:  headers: Record<string, string>,
560:  storageAuth?: CloudStorageAuth,
561:): string {
562:  const repr = representationKey(headers);
563:  // The EFFECTIVE cloud principal (explicit storageAuth MERGED with env creds/
564:  // endpoint/region), not just explicit storageAuth — so an env-authenticated cloud
565:  // entry can't be served later under different/absent creds (#467 P1-B).
566:  const cloud = supportsCloudDownload(url) ? cloudPrincipalKey(url, storageAuth) : "";
567:  // Namespaced for BOTH authed and unauthed so neither can collide with a legacy
568:  // bare-URL entry of unknown auth provenance (#467 P1-C). Representation-affecting
569:  // HTTP headers AND cloud credentials each add a line so different auth can never
570:  // share bytes (#467 P1-2). Empty discriminators are omitted, so unauthenticated
571:  // public downloads stay stable under the v2 namespace.
572:  let id = `${CACHE_NS}\n${url}`;
573:  if (repr) id += `\n${repr}`;
574:  if (cloud) id += `\ncloud:${cloud}`;
575:  return id;
576:}
577:
578:function cachePathForUrl(
579:  url: string,
580:  headers: Record<string, string> = {},
581:  storageAuth?: CloudStorageAuth,
582:): string {
583:  const hash = createHash("sha256")
584:    .update(cacheIdentity(url, headers, storageAuth))
585:    .digest("hex")
586:    .slice(0, HASH_CHARS);
587:  let extension = "";
588:  try {
589:    extension = extname(basename(new URL(url).pathname));
590:  } catch {
591:    // Callers validate URLs before reaching this layer; keep the cache helper
592:    // defensive so fallback direct downloads can still surface the real error.
593:  }
594:  return join(cacheDir(), `${hash}${extension}`);
595:}
596:
597:async function touch(path: string): Promise<void> {
598:  const now = new Date();
599:  await downloadCacheFs.utimes(path, now, now);
600:}
601:
602:/** A parsed resume sidecar: the change-detection validator (ETag / X-Linked-Etag /
603: *  Last-Modified) and, when the first attempt knew it, the AUTHORITATIVE full-file
604: *  size. The size lets a later resume reject a 206 whose Content-Range total
605: *  DISAGREES with what the original response declared — a server that understates
606: *  the total on resume (e.g. `bytes 4-7/8` for a file first seen as 4096) can no
607: *  longer finalize a short prefix as complete (#467). */
608:interface ResumeSidecar {
609:  validator: string;
610:  total?: number;
611:}
612:
613:/** Read the resume sidecar (line 1 = validator, optional line 2 = total) or null.
614: *  Best-effort: a missing/unreadable sidecar just means "resume without an If-Range
615: *  guard" — never fatal. Backward compatible with pre-total single-line sidecars. */
616:async function readValidatorSidecar(path: string): Promise<ResumeSidecar | null> {
617:  try {
618:    const raw = await readFile(path, "utf-8");
619:    const lines = raw.split("\n");
620:    const validator = (lines[0] ?? "").trim();
621:    if (validator.length === 0) return null;
622:    const total = lines.length > 1 ? Number(lines[1].trim()) : NaN;
623:    return Number.isFinite(total) && total > 0 ? { validator, total } : { validator };
624:  } catch {
625:    return null;
626:  }
627:}
628:
629:/** Persist the resume validator (+ authoritative total when known) next to a
630: *  .partial. Best-effort — a failure here only costs the change-detection guard on
631: *  a later resume. The validator is a single-line header value; the optional total
632: *  goes on line 2. */
633:async function writeValidatorSidecar(path: string, value: string, total?: number): Promise<void> {
634:  try {
635:    const body = typeof total === "number" && total > 0 ? `${value}\n${total}` : value;
636:    await writeFile(path, body, "utf-8");
637:  } catch {
638:    /* best effort */
639:  }
640:}
641:
642:/** The strongest resume validator a response can offer, preferring Hugging
643: *  Face's content-addressed `X-Linked-Etag` (the LFS/Xet object hash — carried
644: *  on the huggingface.co/resolve 302, NOT on the CAS CDN's final 200) over a
645: *  plain ETag, falling back to Last-Modified. Capturing X-Linked-Etag from the
646: *  redirect is what lets HF Xet downloads persist a sidecar at all (#467): the
647: *  final CAS response returns neither ETag nor Last-Modified, so without this a
648: *  Xet partial could NEVER be safely resumed. */
649:function extractValidator(res: Response): string | null {
650:  return (
651:    res.headers.get("x-linked-etag") ||
652:    res.headers.get("etag") ||
653:    res.headers.get("last-modified")
654:  );
655:}
656:
657:async function streamUrlToFile(
658:  url: string,
659:  targetPath: string,
660:  headers: Record<string, string>,
661:  logUrl = redactUrlForLogs(url),
662:  storageAuth: CloudStorageAuth = {},
663:  resumeFromBytes = 0,
664:  progress?: ProgressMeta,
665:  resumable = false,
666:  /** Sink for THIS physical download's resume decision, threaded from the job so
667:   *  the outcome is stored on that job — never in a shared keyed map that could
668:   *  misattribute it to another job (#467). Absent for internal/direct callers. */
669:  onResume?: ResumeReporter,
670:  /** Extension of the FINAL destination (e.g. ".safetensors"), NOT of the .partial
671:   *  we stream into. When it's a binary-model extension the completed payload is
672:   *  validated as a real model — an HTML/JSON auth/error body is rejected rather
673:   *  than finalized as a corrupt model under a false success (#473). Empty ⇒ no
674:   *  payload validation (unknown/non-model destination). */
675:  modelExt = "",
676:): Promise<void> {
677:  if (supportsCloudDownload(url)) {
678:    // Cloud downloaders (S3/Azure) don't range-resume — they overwrite the target.
679:    // If a partial exists it's being discarded; surface that (#467) instead of a
680:    // silent restart. Truncate it OURSELVES first and CONFIRM (throw on failure)
681:    // BEFORE reporting discarded:true — the cloud SDKs validate the request/body
682:    // before opening their write stream, so reporting pre-download could otherwise
683:    // claim a discard that a later auth/network failure never performed (P1-1).
684:    if (resumable && resumeFromBytes > 0) {
685:      try {
686:        await writeFile(targetPath, "");
687:      } catch (err) {
688:        throw new ModelError(
689:          `Download restart failed: could not truncate the stale ${resumeFromBytes}-byte partial ` +
690:            `before a cloud re-download. Retry (a fresh attempt restarts from 0).`,
691:          { url: logUrl, cause: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) },
692:        );
693:      }
694:      logger.warn(
695:        `Discarded a ${resumeFromBytes}-byte partial download and restarting from 0: cloud ` +
696:          `downloads (S3/Azure) don't support byte-range resume, so the partial was overwritten — ` +
697:          `re-downloading in full.`,
698:        { url: logUrl, discardedBytes: resumeFromBytes },
699:      );
700:      onResume?.({ outcome: "declined:full-response", discardedBytes: resumeFromBytes, discarded: true });
701:    }
702:    await downloadCloudUrlToFile(url, targetPath, storageAuth);
703:    // #343 edge: the S3/Azure path bypasses the HTTP size gate below. The cloud
704:    // downloaders verify their own Content-Length (truncation), but a stream
705:    // that yielded ZERO bytes can still resolve cleanly — backstop it here so a
706:    // 0-byte cloud object can never be reported as a successful download.
707:    const actual = await fileSizeOrUndefined(targetPath);
708:    if (actual === 0) {
709:      await safeRm(targetPath);
710:      throw new ModelError(

735:  // Resumable downloads: when a partial file exists at targetPath we ask the
736:  // server for the remaining bytes via Range. If the server returns 206 with
737:  // matching Content-Range we append; if it returns 200 (range unsupported,
738:  // or the file changed upstream) we truncate and restart. Idea from
739:  // josephoibrahim/comfy-cozy.
740:  //
741:  // #343 edge: a plain Range resume is unsafe if the upstream file CHANGED
742:  // between the two calls — we would append fresh bytes onto a stale prefix and
743:  // the size check could still pass, producing a corrupt file. Guard it with a
744:  // conditional resume: we persist the first response's validator (ETag, else
745:  // Last-Modified) in a sidecar next to the .partial and replay it as `If-Range`
746:  // on resume. Per RFC 9110 the server then returns 206 (append) ONLY if the
747:  // resource is byte-for-byte unchanged, otherwise 200 with the full body (which
748:  // our append-vs-truncate logic below restarts cleanly). Belt-and-braces, we
749:  // also validate the 206 Content-Range starts exactly at our resume offset.
750:  const validatorSidecar = `${targetPath}.etag`;
751:  let currentUrl = url;
752:  let currentHeaders = headers;
753:  // How many bytes we will actually attempt to resume from. A resume is only
754:  // SAFE when we can prove the upstream file is unchanged, which requires the
755:  // validator (ETag/Last-Modified) we persisted on the first attempt. Without a
756:  // sidecar — a stale partial from a pre-fix build, or a server that sent no
757:  // validator — we cannot detect a changed file, so we must NOT append: fall
758:  // back to a clean restart (Range omitted, the "w" flag truncates the partial).
759:  let effectiveResume = resumeFromBytes;

761:  // response to distinguish a taken resume (206) from an If-Range MISS (200 =
762:  // upstream changed) so we can surface WHY the partial was discarded (#467).
763:  let requestedResume = false;
764:  // Set when a partial existed but no sidecar validator did, so we declined to

766:  // successful response), so a fetch/redirect/status failure before then can't
767:  // falsely claim the partial was discarded (#467 codex round 4).
768:  let resumeDeclinedNoValidator = false;

770:  // we can re-compare it to the value the resolve redirect reports NOW, and
771:  // refuse the append ourselves if they differ (belt-and-braces on top of the
772:  // origin's If-Range: we never trust a CAS 206 whose upstream object changed).
773:  let priorValidator: string | null = null;

780:    priorValidator = sidecar?.validator ?? null;
