1125:      logger.warn(
1126:        `Discarded a ${resumeFromBytes}-byte partial download and restarting from 0: no ` +
1127:          `ETag/Last-Modified validator was ever persisted for it, so a safe resume can't be ` +
1128:          `verified (common on Hugging Face's Xet/CAS CDN, which omits both headers on the file ` +
1129:          `body). This is the safety-first behavior — an unverifiable resume risks a corrupt file ` +
1130:          `(#343). Future downloads capture the validator from the resolve redirect so they CAN resume.`,
1131:        { url: logUrl, discardedBytes: resumeFromBytes },
1132:      );
1133:      onResume?.({ outcome: "declined:no-validator", discardedBytes: resumeFromBytes, discarded: true });
1134:    }
1135:    // The file is now confirmed truncated (we threw otherwise), so a new validator
1136:    // can never pair with a stale prefix (#343). Prefer the final response's
1137:    // validator; fall back to one captured off the redirect chain (HF Xet: the CAS
1138:    // 200 has none, but the resolve 302 carried X-Linked-Etag). Persist the
1139:    // AUTHORITATIVE full-file size (this restart response is a full 200, so its
1140:    // Content-Length IS the total) so a later resume can reject a 206 that
1141:    // understates it (#467).
1142:    const validator = extractValidator(res) || redirectValidator;
1143:    // Authoritative total: the GREATER of Content-Length and HF's X-Linked-Size, so
1144:    // a later resume cross-checks against the TRUE size, not an understated one (#467).
1145:    const fullTotal =
1146:      Math.max(Number(res.headers.get("content-length")) || 0, redirectSize ?? 0) || undefined;
1147:    if (validator) await writeValidatorSidecar(validatorSidecar, validator, fullTotal);
1148:  } else if (appendMode) {
1149:    // A resume was actually taken (validated 206 append). Record it so
1150:    // download_status can report the partial was reused, not discarded (#467).
1151:    onResume?.({ outcome: "resumed", discardedBytes: 0, discarded: false });
1152:  }
1153:
1154:  const nodeStream = Readable.fromWeb(res.body as import("node:stream/web").ReadableStream);
1155:  const fileStream = createWriteStream(targetPath, { flags });
1156:
1157:  // Truncation/verification target = the true full-file size. For a validated 206
1158:  // that is the Content-Range total (NOT content-length, which is only the
1159:  // remaining slice and would mask a short 206). For a fresh/restart 200 it is the
1160:  // GREATER of the response's Content-Length and Hugging Face's authoritative
1161:  // X-Linked-Size — so a CDN that UNDERSTATES Content-Length can't finalize a short
1162:  // body as complete (assertComplete then requires the full X-Linked-Size) (#467).
1163:  const lengthHeader = Number(res.headers.get("content-length") || 0);
1164:  const fresh200Total = Math.max(lengthHeader > 0 ? lengthHeader : 0, redirectSize ?? 0);
1165:  const expectedTotal = appendMode ? (rangeTotal ?? 0) : fresh200Total;
1166:
1167:  // A pipeline() can resolve on a stream that ended EARLY (server dropped the
1168:  // connection, proxy cut it, disk edge case) — leaving a 0-byte or truncated
1169:  // file that would otherwise be reported as a successful download (#343: silent
1170:  // model corruption). Verify the written size before we ever return success.
1171:  const assertComplete = async (): Promise<void> => {
1172:    let actual: number | undefined;
1173:    try {
1174:      const st = await stat(targetPath);
1175:      actual = typeof st?.size === "number" ? st.size : undefined;
1176:    } catch {
1177:      actual = undefined;
1178:    }
1179:    // Couldn't read the written size. FAIL CLOSED when we have an authoritative
1180:    // expected total to check against (#467 P1-B): a transient stat failure must
1181:    // NOT let an early/oversized body be finalized (renamed into cache) as a
1182:    // success — that is exactly the silent-corruption class #343 guards. Keep the
1183:    // partial on disk (it may still be range-resumable) and error instead. Only
1184:    // when NO expected total is known (server sent no Content-Length/Content-Range)
1185:    // do we return — there is nothing to verify against, so a missing size can't
1186:    // prove corruption and mustn't block the download.
1187:    if (actual === undefined) {
1188:      if (expectedTotal > 0) {
1189:        throw new ModelError(
1190:          `Download could not be verified: expected ${expectedTotal} bytes but the written file size ` +
1191:            `couldn't be read — not finalizing (the file may be incomplete). Retry.`,
1192:          { url: logUrl },
1193:        );
1194:      }
1195:      return;
1196:    }
1197:    if (actual === 0) {
1198:      // Nothing landed — remove it (and its validator sidecar) so it can't
1199:      // masquerade as a real file / poison a resume with a validator that has
1200:      // no matching bytes. Best-effort: never let cleanup throw here.
1201:      await safeRm(targetPath);
1202:      if (resumable) await safeRm(validatorSidecar);
1203:      throw new ModelError(
1204:        "Download produced a 0-byte file — the source sent no data. Removed it; retry.",
1205:        { url: logUrl },
1206:      );
1207:    }
1208:    if (expectedTotal > 0 && actual < expectedTotal) {
1209:      // Truncated. Keep the partial on disk so a later call can range-resume it,
1210:      // but do NOT report this as a completed download.
1211:      throw new ModelError(
1212:        `Download truncated: wrote ${actual} of ${expectedTotal} bytes — the stream ended early. Not complete; retry to resume.`,
1213:        { url: logUrl },
1214:      );
1215:    }
1216:    if (expectedTotal > 0 && actual > expectedTotal) {
1217:      // OVERSIZED — the server streamed MORE than the authoritative size (a 206
1218:      // Content-Range total, or a 200 Content-Length). A validated resume that
1219:      // claims `bytes 4-7/8` but streams extra bytes would otherwise finalize a
1220:      // corrupt file with no error (#467 P0-1). This is NOT resumable — the bytes
1221:      // on disk are wrong — so remove the partial + validator and fail; a retry
1222:      // starts clean rather than range-resuming a corrupt prefix.
1223:      await safeRm(targetPath);
1224:      if (resumable) await safeRm(validatorSidecar);
1225:      throw new ModelError(
1226:        `Download oversized: wrote ${actual} bytes but the file is only ${expectedTotal} — the ` +
1227:          `response sent more data than its declared size (corrupt or misbehaving server). Removed ` +
1228:          `the bad file; retry.`,
1229:        { url: logUrl },
1230:      );
1231:    }
1232:  };
1233:
1234:  // Reject a completed body that is an HTML/JSON auth/error page rather than a
1235:  // model file (#473). Runs AFTER assertComplete (size is right) but BEFORE the
1236:  // .partial is renamed into place / the sidecar is dropped — a plausibly-sized
1237:  // login page or `{"error":...}` would otherwise pass the size gate and finalize
1238:  // as a corrupt model under a green success. Removes the bad partial + sidecar so
1239:  // a retry starts clean, and surfaces an actionable (CivitAI-aware) error.
1240:  const assertModelPayload = (): Promise<void> =>
1241:    assertModelPayloadOrThrow({
1242:      targetPath,
1243:      modelExt,
1244:      contentType: res.headers.get("content-type") || "",
1245:      url,
1246:      logUrl,
1247:      // Remove the rejected partial + its resume sidecar, and if removal fails,
1248:      // NEUTRALIZE the partial (truncate to 0) so a retry can't resume onto the
1249:      // poisoned HTML/JSON prefix (#473 P1). safeRm alone would swallow an EPERM and
1250:      // leave a resumable poisoned partial behind.
1251:      onReject: () =>
1252:        discardRejectedPayload(targetPath, resumable ? validatorSidecar : undefined, logUrl),
1253:    });
1254:
1255:  // No progress wanted (internal/cache caller, or not under the panel) → straight pipe.
1256:  if (!progress) {
1257:    await pipeline(nodeStream, fileStream);
1258:    await assertComplete();
1259:    await assertModelPayload();
1260:    // Complete: the validator sidecar is only needed to guard a resume, so drop it.
1261:    if (resumable) await safeRm(validatorSidecar);
1262:    return;
1263:  }
1264:
1265:  // Tally bytes as they flow and report throughput to the panel tray.
1266:  const total = expectedTotal;
1267:  let downloaded = appendMode ? effectiveResume : 0;
1268:  let windowStart = Date.now();
1269:  let windowBytes = downloaded;
1270:  let bytesPerSec = 0;
1271:  const emit = (status: DownloadProgress["status"], force = false) =>
1272:    reportDownloadProgress(
1273:      { id: progress.id, name: progress.name, downloaded, total, bytes_per_sec: bytesPerSec, status },
1274:      force,
1275:    );
1276:  emit("downloading", true); // show the row immediately, even before the first chunk
1277:  const counter = new Transform({
1278:    transform(chunk: Buffer, _enc, cb) {
1279:      downloaded += chunk.length;
1280:      const now = Date.now();

 exited 1 in 175ms:
781:    priorTotal = sidecar?.total;
782:    if (priorValidator) {
783:      currentHeaders = {
784:        ...currentHeaders,
785:        Range: `bytes=${resumeFromBytes}-`,
786:        "If-Range": priorValidator,
787:      };
788:      requestedResume = true;
789:    } else {
790:      // No trustworthy validator → we will discard the un-verifiable partial and
791:      // restart (the deliberate #343 safety fallback). This USED to be silent: a
792:      // multi-GB HF Xet partial (whose CAS CDN sent no ETag/Last-Modified, so no
793:      // sidecar was ever written) got thrown away and re-downloaded from 0 with
794:      // no log and no signal (#467). Defer the log/diagnostic until we actually
795:      // truncate (below), so a pre-write failure can't falsely report a discard.
796:      effectiveResume = 0;
797:      if (resumable) resumeDeclinedNoValidator = true;
798:    }
799:  }
800:  // The content-addressed validator captured from the resolve REDIRECT (HF's
801:  // 302 carries X-Linked-Etag — the file's LFS/Xet content hash — even though
802:  // the final CAS 200 carries no validator). Strictly X-Linked-Etag: a generic
803:  // ETag/Last-Modified on a 3xx describes the redirect/pointer resource, NOT the
804:  // target file, so it must never be promoted to the file's validator. Used
805:  // both as the sidecar fallback (so Xet downloads become resumable) AND as the
806:  // resume-time change check below (#467).
807:  let redirectValidator: string | null = null;
808:  // Set if ANY hop in the redirect chain reports a content-addressed X-Linked-Etag
809:  // that DIFFERS from the validator the partial was written against — proof the
810:  // upstream object changed, even if a later/earlier hop happens to match. Closes
811:  // the multi-hop hole where only the first (or last) value is inspected (#467).
812:  let sawChangedRedirectValidator = false;
813:  // Did the bytes ultimately come from a DIFFERENT origin than we requested (HF
814:  // resolve → CAS CDN)? A cross-origin 206 can't lean on the requesting origin's
815:  // If-Range — the CDN may honor a stale Range and 206 a CHANGED object — so a
816:  // cross-origin resume append MUST independently prove the content is unchanged
817:  // via the content-addressed X-Linked-Etag (#467/#343).
818:  let crossOriginRedirect = false;
819:  // The AUTHORITATIVE full-file size Hugging Face declares on the resolve redirect
820:  // (X-Linked-Size — the true LFS/Xet object size), captured because the final CAS
821:  // 200's Content-Length can UNDERSTATE it (a truncating proxy/CDN). Used as the
822:  // expected total so a short body fails verification instead of finalizing as
823:  // complete — the fresh-download analogue of the resume total cross-check (#467).
824:  let redirectSize: number | undefined;
825:  let res: Response;
826:  for (let redirectCount = 0; ; redirectCount += 1) {
827:    res = await fetchOrThrow(
828:      currentUrl,
829:      { headers: currentHeaders, redirect: "manual" },
830:      currentUrl === url ? logUrl : redactUrlForLogs(currentUrl),
831:    );
832:    if (res.status < 300 || res.status >= 400) break;
833:
834:    // Capture the content-addressed resume validator off the redirect itself.
835:    // HF's resolve URL 302s to the CAS CDN and carries X-Linked-Etag (the LFS/Xet
836:    // object hash) on the 302; the final CAS 200 carries NO validator. Without
837:    // this, Xet downloads never persisted a sidecar and could never resume (#467).
838:    // ONLY X-Linked-Etag — a generic ETag on a 3xx is the pointer's, not the file's.
839:    // Keep the LAST value seen (nearest the final object) for the sidecar/match,
840:    // AND flag if ANY hop's value contradicts the persisted validator on a resume.
841:    const hopValidator = res.headers.get("x-linked-etag");
842:    if (hopValidator) {
843:      redirectValidator = hopValidator;
844:      if (requestedResume && priorValidator && hopValidator !== priorValidator) {
845:        sawChangedRedirectValidator = true;
846:      }
847:    }
848:    // Capture the authoritative object size the redirect declares (last non-empty).
849:    const hopSize = Number(res.headers.get("x-linked-size"));
850:    if (Number.isFinite(hopSize) && hopSize > 0) redirectSize = hopSize;
851:
852:    if (redirectCount >= MAX_HTTP_REDIRECTS) {
853:      throw new ModelError(
854:        `Download redirect limit exceeded (${MAX_HTTP_REDIRECTS}) — the model host kept ` +
855:          `redirecting (Hugging Face routes resolve URLs through the Xet/CAS CDN; a loop ` +
856:          `here usually means a broken mirror or a proxy rewriting the redirect chain).`,
857:        {
858:          url: redactUrlForLogs(currentUrl),
859:          status: res.status,
860:        },
861:      );
862:    }
863:
864:    const location = res.headers.get("location");
865:    if (!location) {
866:      // A 3xx with no Location can't be followed. HF's Xet/CAS flow ALWAYS
867:      // includes a Location on its resolve redirect, so a missing one means a
868:      // proxy stripped it or the host mis-responded — say so instead of the
869:      // confusing "Download failed: 302 Found" the !res.ok path would emit.
870:      throw new ModelError(
871:        `Download failed: the model host returned a ${res.status} redirect with no ` +
872:          `Location header, so it can't be followed. For Hugging Face this usually means a ` +
873:          `proxy stripped the redirect to the Xet/CAS CDN — check any corporate proxy, or ` +
874:          `set HF_ENDPOINT to a reachable mirror and retry.`,
875:        { url: redactUrlForLogs(currentUrl), status: res.status },
876:      );
877:    }
878:
879:    let nextUrl: string;
880:    try {
881:      nextUrl = new URL(location, currentUrl).toString();
882:    } catch {
883:      throw new ModelError(
884:        `Download failed: the model host returned a ${res.status} redirect to an invalid ` +
885:          `Location ("${location}"). The redirect chain can't be followed — retry, or set ` +
886:          `HF_ENDPOINT to a reachable mirror if this is a Hugging Face URL.`,
887:        {
888:          url: redactUrlForLogs(currentUrl),
889:          status: res.status,
890:        },
891:      );
892:    }
893:    // Drop credential-bearing headers (Authorization etc.) when the redirect
894:    // crosses origins. HF's resolve URL 302s to a *pre-signed* Xet/CAS URL on a
895:    // different host (e.g. cas-bridge.xethub.hf.co) that needs no auth — and
896:    // forwarding our HF Bearer token to a third-party CDN would leak it. This
897:    // matches how huggingface_hub follows the CAS redirect. BUT keep Range /
898:    // If-Range: they are not credentials, and the pre-signed CAS URL supports
899:    // byte-range requests — dropping them meant a resume's Range never reached
900:    // the CDN, so HF Xet resumes always fell back to a full re-download (#467).
901:    const sameOrigin = new URL(nextUrl).origin === new URL(currentUrl).origin;
902:    currentUrl = nextUrl;
903:    if (!sameOrigin) {
904:      crossOriginRedirect = true;
905:      const preserved: Record<string, string> = {};
906:      if (currentHeaders.Range) preserved.Range = currentHeaders.Range;
907:      if (currentHeaders["If-Range"]) preserved["If-Range"] = currentHeaders["If-Range"];
908:      currentHeaders = preserved;
909:    }
910:  }
911:
912:  if (!res.ok) {
913:    // Civitai requires an account token for ALL downloads (401 keyless, 403
914:    // for gated/early-access) — a raw status code leaves agents flailing
915:    // through retries (live E2E), so name the fix.
916:    const civitaiAuthHint =
917:      (res.status === 401 || res.status === 403) && /(^|\.)civitai\.com$/i.test(new URL(currentUrl).hostname)
918:        ? " — CivitAI requires an API token for downloads. Set CIVITAI_API_TOKEN (panel Settings › “Set CivitAI token…”, or the env var; create one at civitai.com/user/account) and retry. Do NOT retry other model ids — they will all fail the same way until a token is set."
919:        : "";
920:    throw new ModelError(
921:      `Download failed: ${res.status} ${res.statusText}${civitaiAuthHint}`,
922:      { url: currentUrl === url ? logUrl : redactUrlForLogs(currentUrl), status: res.status },
923:    );
924:  }
925:
926:  if (!res.body) {
927:    throw new ModelError("Download response has no body", { url: logUrl });
928:  }
929:
930:  // #467/#343 belt-and-braces on a 206 RESUME APPEND. A same-origin 206 is safe:
931:  // the very server that evaluated our If-Range is the one serving the bytes, so
932:  // a 206 already proves the resource is unchanged. A CROSS-ORIGIN 206 (HF resolve
933:  // → CAS CDN) cannot lean on that — the CDN may honor a stale Range and 206 a
934:  // CHANGED object regardless of the origin's If-Range — so we must independently
935:  // prove the object is unchanged via the content-addressed X-Linked-Etag: it MUST
936:  // be present AND equal the validator the partial was written against. We check
937:  // BOTH the values captured off the 3xx redirects AND the FINAL response's own
938:  // X-Linked-Etag — a matching redirect followed by a final CDN 206 carrying a
939:  // DIFFERENT content hash must NOT slip through (#467 P0-2). We refuse any 206
940:  // whose observed validators PROVE a change (present but different), cross-origin
941:  // or not. Only 206s are gated — a 200 is a full body and restarts cleanly below.
942:  // On refusal, drop the partial + sidecar so a retry is a clean full download.
943:  if (requestedResume && res.status === 206) {
944:    // The final response's OWN content-addressed validator (the CAS 206 usually
945:    // omits it, but when present it describes exactly THESE bytes — authoritative).
946:    const finalValidator = res.headers.get("x-linked-etag");
947:    // Any content-addressed validator we observed that CONTRADICTS the partial's.
948:    const provenChange =
949:      sawChangedRedirectValidator ||
950:      (redirectValidator !== null && redirectValidator !== priorValidator) ||
951:      (finalValidator !== null && finalValidator !== priorValidator);
952:    // The validator that best binds THESE bytes: the final response's own, else
953:    // the nearest redirect's. Used for the cross-origin "must be proven" check.
954:    const boundValidator = finalValidator ?? redirectValidator;
955:    const unprovenCrossOrigin = crossOriginRedirect && boundValidator !== priorValidator; // includes missing
956:    if (provenChange || unprovenCrossOrigin) {
957:      // provenChange (a validator we saw DIFFERS from the partial's) is a proven
958:      // change; unprovenCrossOrigin alone (no validator to compare) is merely
959:      // UNVERIFIABLE — report each honestly rather than always "changed".
960:      const why = provenChange
961:        ? "the upstream now reports a DIFFERENT content-addressed object (X-Linked-Etag changed" +
962:          (finalValidator !== null && finalValidator !== priorValidator
963:            ? " on the final response"
964:            : " on a redirect hop") +
965:          ")"
966:        : "the resume crossed origins to a CDN that returned no content-addressed validator, so an unchanged upstream can't be proven";
967:      // Remove the stale partial + sidecar, then CONFIRM the partial is actually
968:      // gone (safeRm swallows failures) before claiming it — a swallowed rm
969:      // failure must not be reported as "removed", and would otherwise leave a
970:      // partial a retry re-hits (#467 P1-a). The declined outcome is accurate
971:      // regardless (we refused to append); only the removal wording is conditional.
972:      await safeRm(targetPath);
973:      await safeRm(validatorSidecar);
974:      const removed = await partialConfirmedDiscarded(targetPath);
975:      onResume?.({
976:        outcome: provenChange ? "declined:etag-changed" : "declined:unverifiable",
977:        discardedBytes: resumeFromBytes,
978:        discarded: removed,
979:      });
980:      const tail = removed
981:        ? "Removed the stale partial so a retry restarts cleanly."
982:        : "Could not remove the stale partial — a retry may repeat this rejection; delete the .partial manually if so.";
983:      logger.warn(
984:        `Refusing to append a 206 and ${removed ? "discarded" : "abandoning"} a ${resumeFromBytes}-byte ` +
985:          `partial: ${why} — appending would risk corrupting the file (#343). ${tail}`,
986:        { url: logUrl, discardedBytes: resumeFromBytes, partialRemoved: removed },
987:      );
988:      throw new ModelError(
989:        `Download resume rejected: ${why}. ${tail}`,
990:        { url: logUrl },
991:      );
992:    }
993:  }
994:
995:  // A 206 to a request we did NOT range (a FRESH download, or a no-validator
996:  // decline — effectiveResume === 0, no Range sent) is UNSOLICITED and unsafe: its
997:  // body is only a partial slice, but the "w"/Content-Length path below would
998:  // finalize that short prefix as a complete file (e.g. `bytes 0-1023/4096` with
999:  // Content-Length 1024 → a 1 KiB file renamed into cache as the whole 4096-byte
1000:  // model). Refuse it — a no-Range request must be answered with 200 (#467 P0).
1001:  if (res.status === 206 && effectiveResume === 0) {
1002:    await safeRm(targetPath);
1003:    if (resumable) await safeRm(validatorSidecar);
1004:    const cleared = await partialConfirmedDiscarded(targetPath);
1005:    throw new ModelError(
1006:      `Download failed: the server returned "206 Partial Content" to a request that sent NO Range ` +
1007:        `header. An unsolicited partial response can't be finalized as a complete file (it would ` +
1008:        `silently truncate the model). ${cleared ? "Removed any partial; retry." : "Could not remove the partial — delete it manually and retry."}`,
1009:      { url: logUrl, status: res.status },
1010:    );
1011:  }
1012:
1013:  // Decide append vs truncate based on the response. We only append when we
1014:  // actually asked for a range (effectiveResume > 0, i.e. we had a validator)
1015:  // AND the server honoured it with a 206. Any other 2xx (typically 200) means
1016:  // the server is sending the full file — exactly what If-Range yields when the
1017:  // upstream file changed — so we overwrite and restart, which is correct.
1018:  const appendMode = effectiveResume > 0 && res.status === 206;
1019:
1020:  // The authoritative full-file size, taken from a 206's Content-Range total.
1021:  // Used as the truncation target so a SHORT 206 (a server that satisfies only
1022:  // part of the requested range — RFC 9110 §15.3.7) can't slip through.
1023:  let rangeTotal: number | undefined;
1024:  if (appendMode) {
1025:    // #343 edge: a 206 MUST fully prove where it resumes AND how big the whole
1026:    // file is. Parse Content-Range strictly as `bytes <start>-<end>/<total>` and
1027:    // reject unless: start === our resume offset, end >= start, total is known
1028:    // and consistent (end === total-1, i.e. the range runs to the end of the
1029:    // representation). A missing/malformed/partial range (e.g. `bytes 4-7/1000`
1030:    // returning only 4 of the remaining bytes, or `bytes 4-3/3`, or unanchored
1031:    // garbage) would otherwise let a truncated file be finalized as complete or
1032:    // be appended at the wrong offset. Drop the partial + validator so a retry
1033:    // starts clean rather than compounding the corruption.
1034:    const contentRange = res.headers.get("content-range");
1035:    // Range-unit name is case-insensitive (RFC 9110 §14.1) — accept "Bytes"/"BYTES".
1036:    const m = contentRange ? /^bytes (\d+)-(\d+)\/(\d+)$/i.exec(contentRange.trim()) : null;
1037:    const start = m ? Number(m[1]) : NaN;
1038:    const end = m ? Number(m[2]) : NaN;
1039:    const total = m ? Number(m[3]) : NaN;
1040:    const valid =
1041:      m !== null &&
1042:      start === effectiveResume &&
1043:      end >= start &&
1044:      total > end &&
1045:      end === total - 1;
1046:    if (!valid) {
1047:      await safeRm(targetPath);
1048:      await safeRm(validatorSidecar);
1049:      throw new ModelError(
1050:        `Download resume rejected: a 206 for byte ${effectiveResume}+ must carry a complete, ` +
1051:          `consistent Content-Range "bytes ${effectiveResume}-<end>/<total>" reaching the end of ` +
1052:          `the file, but the server sent ${contentRange ? `"${contentRange}"` : "no Content-Range"}. ` +
1053:          `Refusing to append (would corrupt or truncate the file). Removed the partial; retry.`,
1054:        { url: logUrl, status: res.status },
1055:      );
1056:    }
1057:    // Cross-check the 206's total against the AUTHORITATIVE size the ORIGINAL
1058:    // response declared (persisted in the sidecar). A server that understates the
1059:    // total on resume — e.g. `bytes 4-7/8` for a file first seen as 4096 — would
1060:    // otherwise let a short prefix finalize as "complete" (#467). Refuse the
1061:    // mismatch (also catches a partial we already have that exceeds the new total).
1062:    if (priorTotal !== undefined && total !== priorTotal) {
1063:      await safeRm(targetPath);
1064:      await safeRm(validatorSidecar);
1065:      throw new ModelError(
1066:        `Download resume rejected: the server now reports a total size of ${total} bytes, but the ` +
1067:          `original download recorded ${priorTotal}. The upstream size changed — appending would ` +
1068:          `corrupt or truncate the file. Refusing to append; retry from a clean restart.`,
1069:        { url: logUrl, status: res.status },
1070:      );
1071:    }
1072:    rangeTotal = total;
1073:  }
1074:
1075:  const flags = appendMode ? "a" : "w";
1076:
1077:  // On a fresh/full write (restart) of a resumable target, make the validator
1078:  // and the on-disk bytes failure-atomic: first drop any stale sidecar AND the
1079:  // stale partial (so a crash here leaves NO validator → the next attempt has no
1080:  // sidecar and safely restarts), THEN persist the new validator so that once we
1081:  // begin writing, a truncated partial is already paired with a MATCHING
1082:  // validator. On a 206 append the existing sidecar already matches — leave it.
1083:  if (resumable && !appendMode) {
1084:    // Drop any stale sidecar, then EXPLICITLY truncate the stale partial to zero
1085:    // and verify that truncation SUCCEEDED before we either (a) pair a new
1086:    // validator with the file or (b) report the discard. Doing the truncation
1087:    // ourselves (create-or-truncate to 0) — rather than relying on the lazy "w"
1088:    // stream open below — lets us confirm the old prefix is gone up front. The
1089:    // ordering is the #343 safety invariant: a new validator must NEVER be paired
1090:    // with un-truncated stale bytes (a later If-Range 206 would then append fresh
1091:    // bytes onto a stale prefix and silently corrupt the file). If truncation
1092:    // fails, we write NO validator (a retry sees no sidecar → safe restart) and
1093:    // report nothing (the discard didn't actually happen).
1094:    await safeRm(validatorSidecar);
1095:    // If we can't truncate the stale partial, FAIL rather than fall through to the
1096:    // "w" open below: a partial-truncate failure that the later "w" open then
1097:    // silently fixed would perform the discard WITHOUT reporting it (#467). By
1098:    // throwing here we guarantee the discard is either reported (truncation
1099:    // succeeded) or the download errors (never a silent discard). The sidecar was
1100:    // already removed, so a retry sees no validator → safe restart (#343).
1101:    try {
1102:      await writeFile(targetPath, "");
1103:    } catch (err) {
1104:      throw new ModelError(
1105:        `Download restart failed: could not truncate the stale ${resumeFromBytes}-byte partial to ` +
1106:          `re-download it. Removed its validator; retry (a fresh attempt restarts from 0).`,
1107:        { url: logUrl, cause: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) },
1108:      );
1109:    }
1110:    if (resumeFromBytes > 0 && requestedResume) {
1111:      // Asked to resume (had a validator, sent Range+If-Range) but got a full 200
1112:      // — the upstream changed OR the host doesn't support range resume. The
1113:      // partial is now truncated and the file is being re-downloaded in full.
1114:      logger.warn(
1115:        `Discarded a ${resumeFromBytes}-byte partial download and restarting from 0: the server ` +
1116:          `answered the If-Range resume request with a full ${res.status} instead of a 206 — the ` +
1117:          `upstream file changed, or the host doesn't support resuming — so re-downloading in full.`,
1118:        { url: logUrl, discardedBytes: resumeFromBytes },
1119:      );
1120:      onResume?.({ outcome: "declined:full-response", discardedBytes: resumeFromBytes, discarded: true });
1121:    } else if (resumeFromBytes > 0 && resumeDeclinedNoValidator) {
1122:      // A partial existed but no sidecar validator did, so we never even sent a
1123:      // Range — a safe resume couldn't be verified (common on HF's Xet/CAS CDN,
1124:      // which omits ETag/Last-Modified on the body). Truncated + re-download full.
1125:      logger.warn(
1126:        `Discarded a ${resumeFromBytes}-byte partial download and restarting from 0: no ` +
1127:          `ETag/Last-Modified validator was ever persisted for it, so a safe resume can't be ` +
1128:          `verified (common on Hugging Face's Xet/CAS CDN, which omits both headers on the file ` +
1129:          `body). This is the safety-first behavior — an unverifiable resume risks a corrupt file ` +
1130:          `(#343). Future downloads capture the validator from the resolve redirect so they CAN resume.`,
1131:        { url: logUrl, discardedBytes: resumeFromBytes },
1132:      );
1133:      onResume?.({ outcome: "declined:no-validator", discardedBytes: resumeFromBytes, discarded: true });
1134:    }
1135:    // The file is now confirmed truncated (we threw otherwise), so a new validator
1136:    // can never pair with a stale prefix (#343). Prefer the final response's
1137:    // validator; fall back to one captured off the redirect chain (HF Xet: the CAS
1138:    // 200 has none, but the resolve 302 carried X-Linked-Etag). Persist the
1139:    // AUTHORITATIVE full-file size (this restart response is a full 200, so its
1140:    // Content-Length IS the total) so a later resume can reject a 206 that
1141:    // understates it (#467).
1142:    const validator = extractValidator(res) || redirectValidator;
1143:    // Authoritative total: the GREATER of Content-Length and HF's X-Linked-Size, so
1144:    // a later resume cross-checks against the TRUE size, not an understated one (#467).
1145:    const fullTotal =
1146:      Math.max(Number(res.headers.get("content-length")) || 0, redirectSize ?? 0) || undefined;
1147:    if (validator) await writeValidatorSidecar(validatorSidecar, validator, fullTotal);
1148:  } else if (appendMode) {
1149:    // A resume was actually taken (validated 206 append). Record it so
1150:    // download_status can report the partial was reused, not discarded (#467).
1151:    onResume?.({ outcome: "resumed", discardedBytes: 0, discarded: false });
1152:  }
1153:
1154:  const nodeStream = Readable.fromWeb(res.body as import("node:stream/web").ReadableStream);
1155:  const fileStream = createWriteStream(targetPath, { flags });
1156:
1157:  // Truncation/verification target = the true full-file size. For a validated 206
1158:  // that is the Content-Range total (NOT content-length, which is only the
1159:  // remaining slice and would mask a short 206). For a fresh/restart 200 it is the
1160:  // GREATER of the response's Content-Length and Hugging Face's authoritative
1161:  // X-Linked-Size — so a CDN that UNDERSTATES Content-Length can't finalize a short
1162:  // body as complete (assertComplete then requires the full X-Linked-Size) (#467).
1163:  const lengthHeader = Number(res.headers.get("content-length") || 0);
1164:  const fresh200Total = Math.max(lengthHeader > 0 ? lengthHeader : 0, redirectSize ?? 0);
1165:  const expectedTotal = appendMode ? (rangeTotal ?? 0) : fresh200Total;
1166:
1167:  // A pipeline() can resolve on a stream that ended EARLY (server dropped the
1168:  // connection, proxy cut it, disk edge case) — leaving a 0-byte or truncated
1169:  // file that would otherwise be reported as a successful download (#343: silent
1170:  // model corruption). Verify the written size before we ever return success.
1171:  const assertComplete = async (): Promise<void> => {
1172:    let actual: number | undefined;
1173:    try {
1174:      const st = await stat(targetPath);
1175:      actual = typeof st?.size === "number" ? st.size : undefined;
1176:    } catch {
1177:      actual = undefined;
1178:    }
1179:    // Couldn't read the written size. FAIL CLOSED when we have an authoritative
1180:    // expected total to check against (#467 P1-B): a transient stat failure must
1181:    // NOT let an early/oversized body be finalized (renamed into cache) as a
1182:    // success — that is exactly the silent-corruption class #343 guards. Keep the
1183:    // partial on disk (it may still be range-resumable) and error instead. Only
1184:    // when NO expected total is known (server sent no Content-Length/Content-Range)
1185:    // do we return — there is nothing to verify against, so a missing size can't
1186:    // prove corruption and mustn't block the download.
1187:    if (actual === undefined) {
1188:      if (expectedTotal > 0) {
1189:        throw new ModelError(
1190:          `Download could not be verified: expected ${expectedTotal} bytes but the written file size ` +
1191:            `couldn't be read — not finalizing (the file may be incomplete). Retry.`,
1192:          { url: logUrl },
1193:        );
1194:      }
1195:      return;
1196:    }
1197:    if (actual === 0) {
1198:      // Nothing landed — remove it (and its validator sidecar) so it can't
1199:      // masquerade as a real file / poison a resume with a validator that has
1200:      // no matching bytes. Best-effort: never let cleanup throw here.
1201:      await safeRm(targetPath);
1202:      if (resumable) await safeRm(validatorSidecar);
1203:      throw new ModelError(
1204:        "Download produced a 0-byte file — the source sent no data. Removed it; retry.",
1205:        { url: logUrl },
1206:      );
1207:    }
1208:    if (expectedTotal > 0 && actual < expectedTotal) {
1209:      // Truncated. Keep the partial on disk so a later call can range-resume it,
1210:      // but do NOT report this as a completed download.
1211:      throw new ModelError(
1212:        `Download truncated: wrote ${actual} of ${expectedTotal} bytes — the stream ended early. Not complete; retry to resume.`,
1213:        { url: logUrl },
1214:      );
1215:    }
1216:    if (expectedTotal > 0 && actual > expectedTotal) {
1217:      // OVERSIZED — the server streamed MORE than the authoritative size (a 206
1218:      // Content-Range total, or a 200 Content-Length). A validated resume that
1219:      // claims `bytes 4-7/8` but streams extra bytes would otherwise finalize a
1220:      // corrupt file with no error (#467 P0-1). This is NOT resumable — the bytes
1221:      // on disk are wrong — so remove the partial + validator and fail; a retry
1222:      // starts clean rather than range-resuming a corrupt prefix.
1223:      await safeRm(targetPath);
1224:      if (resumable) await safeRm(validatorSidecar);
1225:      throw new ModelError(
1226:        `Download oversized: wrote ${actual} bytes but the file is only ${expectedTotal} — the ` +
1227:          `response sent more data than its declared size (corrupt or misbehaving server). Removed ` +
1228:          `the bad file; retry.`,
1229:        { url: logUrl },
1230:      );
1231:    }
1232:  };
1233:
1234:  // Reject a completed body that is an HTML/JSON auth/error page rather than a
1235:  // model file (#473). Runs AFTER assertComplete (size is right) but BEFORE the
1236:  // .partial is renamed into place / the sidecar is dropped — a plausibly-sized
1237:  // login page or `{"error":...}` would otherwise pass the size gate and finalize
1238:  // as a corrupt model under a green success. Removes the bad partial + sidecar so
1239:  // a retry starts clean, and surfaces an actionable (CivitAI-aware) error.
1240:  const assertModelPayload = (): Promise<void> =>
1241:    assertModelPayloadOrThrow({
1242:      targetPath,
1243:      modelExt,
1244:      contentType: res.headers.get("content-type") || "",
1245:      url,
1246:      logUrl,
1247:      // Remove the rejected partial + its resume sidecar, and if removal fails,
1248:      // NEUTRALIZE the partial (truncate to 0) so a retry can't resume onto the
1249:      // poisoned HTML/JSON prefix (#473 P1). safeRm alone would swallow an EPERM and
1250:      // leave a resumable poisoned partial behind.
1251:      onReject: () =>
1252:        discardRejectedPayload(targetPath, resumable ? validatorSidecar : undefined, logUrl),
1253:    });
1254:
1255:  // No progress wanted (internal/cache caller, or not under the panel) → straight pipe.
1256:  if (!progress) {
1257:    await pipeline(nodeStream, fileStream);
1258:    await assertComplete();
1259:    await assertModelPayload();
1260:    // Complete: the validator sidecar is only needed to guard a resume, so drop it.
1261:    if (resumable) await safeRm(validatorSidecar);
1262:    return;
1263:  }
1264:
1265:  // Tally bytes as they flow and report throughput to the panel tray.
1266:  const total = expectedTotal;
1267:  let downloaded = appendMode ? effectiveResume : 0;
1268:  let windowStart = Date.now();
1269:  let windowBytes = downloaded;
1270:  let bytesPerSec = 0;
1271:  const emit = (status: DownloadProgress["status"], force = false) =>
1272:    reportDownloadProgress(
1273:      { id: progress.id, name: progress.name, downloaded, total, bytes_per_sec: bytesPerSec, status },
1274:      force,
1275:    );
1276:  emit("downloading", true); // show the row immediately, even before the first chunk
1277:  const counter = new Transform({
1278:    transform(chunk: Buffer, _enc, cb) {
1279:      downloaded += chunk.length;
1280:      const now = Date.now();

exec
"C:\\Windows\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command 'rg -n "''^" src/services/download-cache.ts | Select-Object -Skip 1280 -First 520' in C:\Users\Artokun\code\comfyui-mcp\.claude\worktrees\agent-a357b101a6eb58cf0
 succeeded in 163ms:
1281:      const dt = now - windowStart;
1282:      if (dt >= 400) {
1283:        bytesPerSec = ((downloaded - windowBytes) * 1000) / dt;
1284:        windowStart = now;
1285:        windowBytes = downloaded;
1286:        emit("downloading");
1287:      }
1288:      cb(null, chunk);
1289:    },
1290:  });
1291:  try {
1292:    await pipeline(nodeStream, counter, fileStream);
1293:    await assertComplete();
1294:    await assertModelPayload();
1295:    if (resumable) await safeRm(validatorSidecar);
1296:    bytesPerSec = 0;
1297:    emit("done", true);
1298:  } catch (err) {
1299:    emit("error", true);
1300:    throw err;
1301:  }
1302:}
1303:
1304:async function downloadIntoCache(
1305:  url: string,
1306:  headers: Record<string, string>,
1307:  logUrl?: string,
1308:  storageAuth: CloudStorageAuth = {},
1309:  progress?: ProgressMeta,
1310:  onResume?: ResumeReporter,
1311:  modelExt = "",
1312:): Promise<string> {
1313:  // Representation-aware identity (#467): a same-URL download with different HTTP
1314:  // auth headers OR different cloud (S3/Azure) credentials gets its OWN cache file,
1315:  // partial and in-flight slot — never coalesced onto another caller's stream.
1316:  const target = cachePathForUrl(url, headers, storageAuth);
1317:  const key = target;
1318:
1319:  const existing = inflight.get(key);
1320:  // A job COALESCING onto an in-flight physical download gets no resume decision
1321:  // of its own — the decision is reported to the job that actually runs the
1322:  // stream (#467). This is inherent to the callback model: onResume is not passed
1323:  // to the shared promise, so a coalesced caller simply awaits the same result.
1324:  if (existing) return existing;
1325:
1326:  const promise = (async () => {
1327:    await downloadCacheFs.mkdir(cacheDir(), { recursive: true });
1328:
1329:    try {
1330:      const info = await downloadCacheFs.stat(target);
1331:      if (info.isFile()) {
1332:        // #343 edge: never serve a 0-byte cache entry as a hit. One could exist
1333:        // from an interrupted rename, an older build without the size gate, or
1334:        // external tampering — treat it as a miss and re-download rather than
1335:        // materializing an empty file that reports success.
1336:        if (info.size === 0) {
1337:          await downloadCacheFs.rm(target, { force: true }).catch(() => undefined);
1338:        } else {
1339:          await touch(target);
1340:          // Cache hit ⇒ no resume/discard this attempt; onResume is never called,
1341:          // so the job's resume field stays empty (nothing to surface).
1342:          return target;
1343:        }
1344:      }
1345:    } catch {
1346:      // Cache miss.
1347:    }
1348:
1349:    // Deterministic .partial filename so a crashed/interrupted download
1350:    // resumes from the byte it left off on the next call, rather than
1351:    // restarting from zero. (See streamUrlToFile for the Range + flags
1352:    // handshake.) Cleanup on terminal failure stays unchanged.
1353:    const partial = join(cacheDir(), `.${basename(target)}.partial`);
1354:    let resumeFromBytes = 0;
1355:    try {
1356:      const existing = await downloadCacheFs.stat(partial);
1357:      if (existing.isFile() && existing.size > 0) {
1358:        resumeFromBytes = existing.size;
1359:        logger.info("Resuming partial download", {
1360:          url: logUrl,
1361:          bytes: resumeFromBytes,
1362:        });
1363:      }
1364:    } catch {
1365:      // No partial — fresh download.
1366:    }
1367:
1368:    try {
1369:      await streamUrlToFile(
1370:        url,
1371:        partial,
1372:        headers,
1373:        logUrl,
1374:        storageAuth,
1375:        resumeFromBytes,
1376:        progress,
1377:        true, // resumable: cache partials use the .partial + If-Range resume handshake
1378:        onResume,
1379:        modelExt,
1380:      );
1381:      await downloadCacheFs.rename(partial, target);
1382:      await touch(target);
1383:      return target;
1384:    } catch (err) {
1385:      // Leave the partial on disk for a future resume; only nuke it if it
1386:      // is now empty (server said the previous partial was bogus, or our
1387:      // first write failed).
1388:      try {
1389:        const remaining = await downloadCacheFs.stat(partial);
1390:        if (remaining.size === 0) {
1391:          await downloadCacheFs.rm(partial, { force: true }).catch(() => undefined);
1392:          // The validator sidecar is only meaningful alongside a resumable
1393:          // partial — drop it too so a later attempt doesn't If-Range against a
1394:          // file that no longer exists.
1395:          await downloadCacheFs.rm(`${partial}.etag`, { force: true }).catch(() => undefined);
1396:        }
1397:      } catch {
1398:        // Partial gone — nothing to clean.
1399:      }
1400:      throw err;
1401:    }
1402:  })();
1403:
1404:  inflight.set(key, promise);
1405:  try {
1406:    return await promise;
1407:  } finally {
1408:    inflight.delete(key);
1409:  }
1410:}
1411:
1412:/** A cryptographically-random, unguessable temp path next to `base`. NOT a
1413: *  predictable pid+counter name (#467 P1-A): a predictable name can collide with a
1414: *  temp left by a crashed attempt or a reused PID, and — if that leftover is still
1415: *  hardlinked to a cache inode — a non-exclusive create would truncate it and
1416: *  corrupt that cache entry. Random + exclusive-create makes a collision effectively
1417: *  impossible AND detectable (EEXIST), so we never write over a pre-existing file. */
1418:function randomTempPath(base: string, tag: string): string {
1419:  return `${base}.${tag}-${randomBytes(12).toString("hex")}.tmp`;
1420:}
1421:
1422:/** Reserve an unguessable temp path by creating it EXCLUSIVELY (O_EXCL via the "wx"
1423: *  flag), retrying on the astronomically-unlikely EEXIST. Guarantees the returned
1424: *  path is a FRESH standalone inode — so a subsequent "w" open (streamUrlToFile /
1425: *  the S3/Azure downloaders truncate) writes to our own new file and can NEVER
1426: *  follow a pre-existing hardlink into a cache inode (#467 P1-A). */
1427:async function reserveExclusiveTemp(base: string, tag: string): Promise<string> {
1428:  for (let attempt = 1; ; attempt++) {
1429:    const p = randomTempPath(base, tag);
1430:    try {
1431:      await writeFile(p, "", { flag: "wx" });
1432:      return p;
1433:    } catch (err) {
1434:      if ((err as NodeJS.ErrnoException)?.code === "EEXIST" && attempt < MATERIALIZE_TEMP_ATTEMPTS) {
1435:        continue;
1436:      }
1437:      throw err;
1438:    }
1439:  }
1440:}
1441:
1442:/** True for a hardlink error that means "hardlinks aren't usable here" — the ONLY
1443: *  case where copy-fallback is legitimate. NOT EEXIST (a name collision — retry a
1444: *  new name; copy-falling-back there is what truncates a stale hardlinked temp and
1445: *  poisons a cache inode, #467 P1-A) and NOT other errors (propagate). */
1446:function isHardlinkUnsupported(code: unknown): boolean {
1447:  // ONLY genuine "hardlinks aren't usable here" codes: cross-device (EXDEV), the FS
1448:  // doesn't support links (ENOSYS/EPERM — some Windows/network FS). NOT EACCES — an
1449:  // ACL denial is a real permission error that must propagate, not silently copy
1450:  // (#467 P2).
1451:  return code === "EXDEV" || code === "EPERM" || code === "ENOSYS";
1452:}
1453:
1454:/** Rename `tmp` over `targetPath`. On POSIX, rename atomically replaces an existing
1455: *  destination. Windows can't rename OVER an existing file and returns EPERM/EACCES/
1456: *  EEXIST — ONLY then do we move the destination ASIDE and swap. Any OTHER rename
1457: *  error (ENOENT, EIO, EXDEV, …) must NOT touch a valid existing destination (#467):
1458: *  clean our temp and propagate. */
1459:async function renameTempOverDestination(tmp: string, targetPath: string): Promise<void> {
1460:  try {
1461:    await downloadCacheFs.rename(tmp, targetPath);
1462:    return;
1463:  } catch (err) {
1464:    const code = (err as NodeJS.ErrnoException)?.code;
1465:    const windowsOverwrite =
1466:      process.platform === "win32" &&
1467:      (code === "EPERM" || code === "EACCES" || code === "EEXIST");
1468:    if (!windowsOverwrite) {
1469:      await downloadCacheFs.rm(tmp, { force: true }).catch(() => undefined);
1470:      throw err;
1471:    }
1472:    // Windows: move the existing destination ASIDE to a backup, then swap the temp
1473:    // in. On ANY swap failure, RESTORE the backup so a valid destination is never
1474:    // lost. If even the restore fails, the original is INTACT at `backup` — surface
1475:    // that path in the error so it's recoverable, never silently stranded (#467).
1476:    const backup = `${targetPath}.bak-${randomBytes(9).toString("hex")}.tmp`;
1477:    let backedUp = false;
1478:    try {
1479:      await downloadCacheFs.rename(targetPath, backup);
1480:      backedUp = true;
1481:    } catch {
1482:      /* destination may not exist — nothing to move aside */
1483:    }
1484:    try {
1485:      await downloadCacheFs.rename(tmp, targetPath);
1486:      if (backedUp) await downloadCacheFs.rm(backup, { force: true }).catch(() => undefined);
1487:    } catch (e) {
1488:      await downloadCacheFs.rm(tmp, { force: true }).catch(() => undefined);
1489:      if (backedUp) {
1490:        const restored = await downloadCacheFs
1491:          .rename(backup, targetPath)
1492:          .then(() => true)
1493:          .catch(() => false);
1494:        if (!restored) {
1495:          throw new ModelError(
1496:            `Download could not be finalized and the destination could not be restored — your ` +
1497:              `previous file is PRESERVED at "${backup}"; move it back to "${targetPath}" manually. ` +
1498:              `Cause: ${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}`,
1499:            { url: targetPath },
1500:          );
1501:        }
1502:      }
1503:      throw e;
1504:    }
1505:  }
1506:}
1507:
1508:const MATERIALIZE_TEMP_ATTEMPTS = 5;
1509:
1510:async function materializeCacheFile(
1511:  cachePath: string,
1512:  targetPath: string,
1513:): Promise<"hardlink" | "copy"> {
1514:  if (resolve(cachePath) === resolve(targetPath)) return "hardlink";
1515:
1516:  // Materialize ATOMICALLY into an UNGUESSABLE, EXCLUSIVELY-created temp, then
1517:  // rename over targetPath — never write directly at targetPath, and never reuse a
1518:  // possibly-stale temp name (#467 P1-A). Two jobs materializing DIFFERENT
1519:  // representations to the SAME path can race; building each in its own random temp
1520:  // (a hardlink to, or an EXCLUSIVE copy of, our OWN cache inode) keeps every cache
1521:  // inode read-only here and makes the final swap atomic (last-writer-wins on the
1522:  // destination only, never a cache entry).
1523:  for (let attempt = 1; ; attempt++) {
1524:    const tmp = randomTempPath(targetPath, "mat");
1525:    let mode: "hardlink" | "copy";
1526:    try {
1527:      await downloadCacheFs.link(cachePath, tmp);
1528:      mode = "hardlink";
1529:    } catch (err) {
1530:      const code = (err as NodeJS.ErrnoException)?.code;
1531:      // Name collision → pick a fresh random name (never copy-fall-back onto a
1532:      // possibly-stale, possibly-cache-hardlinked temp).
1533:      if (code === "EEXIST") {
1534:        if (attempt >= MATERIALIZE_TEMP_ATTEMPTS) throw err;
1535:        continue;
1536:      }
1537:      // Only fall back to copy when hardlinks genuinely aren't usable here.
1538:      if (!isHardlinkUnsupported(code)) throw err;
1539:      try {
1540:        // EXCLUSIVE copy: fail (EEXIST) rather than truncate a pre-existing file.
1541:        await downloadCacheFs.copyFile(cachePath, tmp, fsConstants.COPYFILE_EXCL);
1542:      } catch (e) {
1543:        const ecode = (e as NodeJS.ErrnoException)?.code;
1544:        await downloadCacheFs.rm(tmp, { force: true }).catch(() => undefined);
1545:        if (ecode === "EEXIST" && attempt < MATERIALIZE_TEMP_ATTEMPTS) continue;
1546:        throw e;
1547:      }
1548:      mode = "copy";
1549:    }
1550:    await renameTempOverDestination(tmp, targetPath);
1551:    return mode;
1552:  }
1553:}
1554:
1555:async function evictLruIfNeeded(): Promise<void> {
1556:  const limit = cacheSizeLimitBytes();
1557:  if (limit <= 0) return;
1558:
1559:  const dir = cacheDir();
1560:  const entries = await downloadCacheFs.readdir(dir, { withFileTypes: true });
1561:  const files = await Promise.all(
1562:    entries
1563:      .filter((entry) => entry.isFile() && !entry.name.startsWith("."))
1564:      .map(async (entry) => {
1565:        const path = join(dir, entry.name);
1566:        const info = await downloadCacheFs.stat(path);
1567:        return {
1568:          path,
1569:          size: info.size,
1570:          time: Math.max(info.atimeMs, info.mtimeMs),
1571:        };
1572:      }),
1573:  );
1574:
1575:  let total = files.reduce((sum, file) => sum + file.size, 0);
1576:  if (total <= limit) return;
1577:
1578:  files.sort((a, b) => a.time - b.time);
1579:  for (const file of files) {
1580:    await downloadCacheFs.rm(file.path, { force: true });
1581:    total -= file.size;
1582:    if (total <= limit) break;
1583:  }
1584:}
1585:
1586:export async function downloadUrlToFile(
1587:  url: string,
1588:  targetPath: string,
1589:  headers: Record<string, string>,
1590:  logUrl?: string,
1591:  storageAuth: CloudStorageAuth = {},
1592:  progress?: ProgressMeta,
1593:  modelExt = extname(targetPath),
1594:): Promise<void> {
1595:  await streamUrlToFile(
1596:    url,
1597:    targetPath,
1598:    headers,
1599:    logUrl,
1600:    storageAuth,
1601:    0,
1602:    progress,
1603:    false,
1604:    undefined,
1605:    modelExt,
1606:  );
1607:}
1608:
1609:export async function downloadWithCache(
1610:  options: DownloadCacheOptions,
1611:): Promise<DownloadCacheResult> {
1612:  const logUrl = options.logUrl ?? redactUrlForLogs(options.url);
1613:  // The FINAL destination extension drives model-payload validation (#473). Derived
1614:  // from the real targetPath here — NOT from the .partial / random temp we actually
1615:  // stream into (those carry `.partial`/`.tmp`) — and threaded down so the completed
1616:  // body is checked against what the destination IS (a `.safetensors` etc.).
1617:  const modelExt = extname(options.targetPath);
1618:  try {
1619:    const cachePath = await downloadIntoCache(
1620:      options.url,
1621:      options.headers,
1622:      logUrl,
1623:      options.storageAuth,
1624:      options.progress,
1625:      options.onResume,
1626:      modelExt,
1627:    );
1628:    // Validate the CACHE FILE itself before materializing it to this destination —
1629:    // this is the authoritative per-caller gate (#473). It covers three cases the
1630:    // stream-time check can't: (1) a CACHE HIT that skipped streaming entirely,
1631:    // (2) a COALESCED caller whose .safetensors destination consumed a stream that
1632:    // ran under a different (e.g. non-model) caller's modelExt, and (3) a legacy
1633:    // cache entry poisoned before this fix. Content-Type is gone by now, so this is
1634:    // a body-magic-bytes check; the stream-time pass already used Content-Type too.
1635:    await assertModelPayloadOrThrow({
1636:      targetPath: cachePath,
1637:      modelExt,
1638:      contentType: "",
1639:      url: options.url,
1640:      logUrl,
1641:      // Drop the poisoned cache entry so a retry re-downloads clean rather than
1642:      // re-serving the same bad bytes on every call. Truncate-to-0 fallback if rm
1643:      // fails (a 0-byte cache entry is treated as a miss), and log if even that
1644:      // fails, so a poisoned cache entry can't silently re-poison cache hits (#473 P1).
1645:      onReject: () => discardRejectedPayload(cachePath, undefined, logUrl),
1646:    });
1647:    const materializedBy = await materializeCacheFile(cachePath, options.targetPath);
1648:    await evictLruIfNeeded();
1649:    return {
1650:      targetPath: options.targetPath,
1651:      usedCache: true,
1652:      cachePath,
1653:      materializedBy,
1654:    };
1655:  } catch (err) {
1656:    if (err instanceof ModelError) throw err;
1657:    logger.warn("Download cache unavailable; falling back to direct download", {
1658:      url: logUrl,
1659:      error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err),
1660:    });
1661:    // Download to an EXCLUSIVELY-created, unguessable temp then rename over the
1662:    // destination — NEVER stream "w" directly at targetPath (#467). If a concurrent
1663:    // materialize already hardlinked targetPath to a cache inode, a direct "w" open
1664:    // would follow that hardlink and overwrite the OTHER representation's cache
1665:    // entry with these bytes (poison). Reserving the temp with O_EXCL guarantees a
1666:    // fresh standalone inode, so the downloader's "w"/truncate can never follow a
1667:    // pre-existing hardlink; rename only swaps the directory entry. A failed stream
1668:    // leaves the destination untouched.
1669:    const tmp = await reserveExclusiveTemp(options.targetPath, "dl");
1670:    try {
1671:      await downloadUrlToFile(
1672:        options.url,
1673:        tmp,
1674:        options.headers,
1675:        logUrl,
1676:        options.storageAuth,
1677:        options.progress,
1678:        modelExt,
1679:      );
1680:      await renameTempOverDestination(tmp, options.targetPath);
1681:    } catch (e) {
1682:      await downloadCacheFs.rm(tmp, { force: true }).catch(() => undefined);
1683:      throw e;
1684:    }
1685:    return { targetPath: options.targetPath, usedCache: false };
1686:  }
1687:}

+  // A handle whose read() yields `bytes` — used to control what #473's payload sniff sees.
+  function openHandleYielding(bytes: Buffer) {
+    return {
+      read: async (buf: Buffer, off: number, len: number, pos: number) => {
+        if (pos >= bytes.length) return { bytesRead: 0 };
+        const bytesRead = bytes.copy(buf, off, pos, Math.min(pos + len, bytes.length));
+        return { bytesRead };
+      },
+      close: async () => undefined,
+    };
+  }

+/** Remove `path`, returning true iff it is confirmed gone. Unlike `safeRm` this
+ *  LOGS a removal failure (EPERM/EACCES/…) instead of swallowing it silently — a
+ *  left-behind rejected artifact (a poisoned partial / cache entry) must at least be
+ *  visible, since a later cache-hit or resume could otherwise re-hit it (#473 P1). */
+async function rmOrLog(path: string, logUrl: string): Promise<boolean> {
+  try {
+    await rm(path, { force: true });
+    return true;
+  } catch (err) {
+    logger.warn(
+      `Failed to remove a rejected download artifact "${path}" — a retry may re-hit it; ` +
+        `delete it manually if the same rejection repeats.`,
+      { url: logUrl, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) },
+    );
+    return false;
+  }
+}
+
+/** Discard a payload that was REJECTED as a non-model (HTML/JSON auth/error) body.
+ *  Removes the file and its resume sidecar and, CRUCIALLY, if the file itself can't
+ *  be removed, NEUTRALIZES it by truncating to 0 bytes so a retry can neither resume
+ *  onto the poisoned prefix (a 0-byte partial is treated as fresh) nor serve it as a
+ *  cache hit (a 0-byte cache entry is treated as a miss). Surfaces a hard failure via
+ *  the log when it can neither remove nor truncate (#473 P1). */
+async function discardRejectedPayload(
+  path: string,
+  sidecarPath: string | undefined,
+  logUrl: string,
+): Promise<void> {
+  const removed = await rmOrLog(path, logUrl);
+  if (sidecarPath) await rmOrLog(sidecarPath, logUrl);
+  if (removed) return;
+  // Removal failed — neutralize the still-present poisoned bytes so a retry can't
+  // resume onto them or re-serve them as a cache hit.
+  try {
+    await writeFile(path, "");
+  } catch (err) {
+    logger.error(
+      `Could not remove OR truncate a rejected non-model payload "${path}"; a retry may re-serve ` +
+        `or resume it. Delete this file manually.`,
+      { url: logUrl, error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) },
+    );
+  }
+}
+

  *  number, only over a number that proves corruption. */

355:  // A handle whose read() yields `bytes` — used to control what #473's payload sniff sees.
356:  function openHandleYielding(bytes: Buffer) {
357:    return {
358:      read: async (buf: Buffer, off: number, len: number, pos: number) => {
359:        if (pos >= bytes.length) return { bytesRead: 0 };
360:        const bytesRead = bytes.copy(buf, off, pos, Math.min(pos + len, bytes.length));
361:        return { bytesRead };
362:      },
363:      close: async () => undefined,
364:    };
365:  }
